## gate 6 has begun; every style is to be written for compound time; odd meters later (stage 6)

Brandon, 2026-11-16: "i'd say the week has already started - i've been playing along with the app while you've been working, and it seriously sounds incredible". **Gate 6 ("play over it for a week") runs from today.** What he trips on in the week is collected, not guessed at.

"we should probably handle the other styles in compound time as well, and in the future, we should support other odd time signatures fully across genres (even if it's not idiomatic). the band should be smart enough to figure it out".

- **Now**: funk, disco, reggae and neo-soul are each written for 6/8 and 12/8 (`prompts/meter-compound-style.md`, one Opus build a style, two at a time). A style's second compound meter may be spread from its first in its own data where that is how the music goes (the design's word: half a bar of twelve is not a bar of six for anything but the drums' basic beat). After them, the four styles that have one compound meter get the other (jazz in 6/8 and 12/8, the blues and rock in 6/8, acoustic in 12/8).
- **Later, a design of its own (Fable)**: every style in every meter a chart can write (5/4, 7/8, 9/8, 7/4 and the rest), not by a part written for each but by the band working it out from what the style has written for a quarter pulse and for a dotted pulse and how the bar groups. The fit is the start of that and is not yet good enough to be the answer. Not started.
- **The push suite's time is its slowest style's**: the styles run side by side, and rock (17 s alone, with its bars of twelve held at three levels) sets it. A style's own tests should stay under about 14 s alone.
## the ninth under the third (stage 6)

From `prompts/jazz-voicing-ninth-under-third.md`: after D7b5 on Take the "A" Train the jazz piano voiced Dm7 as E4 F4 A4 C5, the 9th a half step under the 3rd on the bottom of the hand.

- **The cause is in the engine, not the style.** The rootless recipe for a minor seventh is 3, 7, 9, 5 in close position (CH-06), so all four inversions are offered and two of them hold the 9th-against-b3 half step: C E F A (the 7th on the bottom, a standard form, the rub covered inside the hand) and E F A C (the 9th on the bottom, Q-03's fault). The critic's `minor-seconds` costs both the same 8, so it cannot tell them apart, and from D7b5's C4 Gb4 Ab4 B4 the hand took E F A C by a quarter of a point (12.8 against C E F A's 13.05: each moves its voices 7 semitones, E F A C's top moves one semitone to C5 and sits two over the target, C E F A's moves two to A4 and sits one under it). F A C E, the 3rd on the bottom, costs 21.6 from F4 and 29.8 from F3 (the scores are the engine's own, `score` over `VOICING_FEATURES` with jazz's weights). The filter that keeps a half step off the bottom while anything else will do applied only under a soloist's line.
- **It was not this tune alone.** Over the eighteen charts jazz plays as its own (its four and the fourteen standards written for it), six seeds and the five kinds of take: 1,289 of 77,777 strikes had a half step on the bottom, nearly all a minor seventh with its 9th under its b3. "A" Train 383 of 4,836 (Dm7 E F A C 332 times); Stella by Starlight 227 of 4,302 (Cm7 D Eb G Bb); All the Things You Are 198 of 5,463 (Bbm7 C Db F Ab); Body and Soul 154 of 5,013 (Bbm7 C Db F Ab); Have You Met Miss Jones 116 of 4,910 (Cm7); Giant Steps 110 of 3,814 (C#m7 Eb E Ab B); Cherokee 97 of 8,773 (Cm7); three in the rest. Read by the chord written under the strike, 1,161 were on minor chords, 67 dominant, 59 major and 2 half-diminished; the last three are almost all a minor seventh played early on the "and" of 4. Every other style had none or one on its own charts.
- **The property is the old one widened, not a new one** ("the ninth under the third anywhere" would forbid C E F A, the standard form the blues piano's weights are tuned to lead to, CH-06): "the hand does not growl: a chord's two lowest notes are a half step apart only on a dominant from E4 up, in every take, under a solo or not" (Q-03, SU-B-10, KF-B-29). It moved from the soloist's file to the piano's and replaces the under-a-solo version. It failed on the engine as it was (first on All the Things You Are, a solo take, seed 2, C4 Db4 F4 Ab4 in the band's chorus).
- **The fix**: the growl filter is applied to every strike, once per chord when its candidates are built, instead of only under the line. A filter only removes candidates that were not chosen unless they were, so the only strikes that change are those that took a growl and what is led from them; every golden take is the same to the byte, and the other seven styles' scorecards did not move. A data fix (jazz's weights) could not have told the two half steps apart, and a new cost for the bottom would have been a price where the notes ask for a rule.
- **What moved in jazz** (`scorecard/jazz.json` accepted): `chords.voicing-faults` mean 1.49% to 0.02%, in target 73% of takes to 100%; `chords.top-holds` 40.3% to 41.7%; `chords.top-steps` 46.3% to 44.9%; `chords.top-leaps` 0.06% to 0.26% of changes (target at most 22%); `chords.lowest-note` 58.96 to 58.91, in target 74% to 73.5%; `chords.closest-to-bass` 9.68 to 9.61. "A" Train alone (`ensemble score library/standard-take-the-a-train.chart --style jazz`, 200 takes): `chords.voicing-faults` mean 7% to 0%, in target 10% to 100%; `chords.top-holds` 35% to 54%; `chords.top-steps` 62% to 42%; `chords.top-movement` 1.11 to 0.91; `chords.lowest-note` 59.83 to 59.22, in target 69% to 68%. Dm7 is now C4 E4 F4 A4 there, and G7 after it B3 E4 F4 A4.
- **Left**: the top leaps that rose are nearly all Body and Soul (115 of 3,720 changes against 3 before): from F7's A3 Db4 Eb4 Gb4 the Bbm7 that was C Db F Ab is now Db4 F4 Ab4 C5, a tritone up on top, because Ab3 C4 Db4 F4 (the 7th on the bottom) sits past the reach under the target and pays `top-out-of-reach`. The notes do not say which of the two a pianist would play there; nobody has heard it. The scorecard still finds a fault in one take in a few hundred (the highest take 2%), not met on the six seeds the properties play; not looked for further.

## small things in the app, 1 (stage 6, during gate 6)

Built to `prompts/app-tidy-1.md`. Nothing in the styles or the audio package changed; in the engine only the MIDI export (item 5). No test added, no journey step changed.

- **1. The way out of the editing mode says "Stop editing"; Done is the dock's alone.** He has used the dock on the keyboard and passed it, and Done there is where a phone's own keyboard puts it (close what is being typed); the top button is the one that changes. "Stop editing" is the plain pair of "Edit", fits the row at 390 px (97 px wide, the row 46 px high as before), and is not "Finish", which is the transport's word for a jam. A sheet's Done is now the only Done on the screen too.
- **2. "Edit a copy" and "Make a copy to edit" make "<title> copy"**, by the same rewrite Duplicate uses. A copy not yet changed is opened again rather than made twice, as before (it is found by its text, now the copy's text). A changed copy and a second one share the title "<title> copy", exactly as two Duplicates of one chart do: Duplicate does not number its copies, so neither does this.
- **3. More in the editing mode has "Meter"**, the browser's own list laid over the line as "Soloist plays" is: 4/4, 3/4, 6/8, 12/8, 6/4, and a meter typed in the text that is not one of them is shown as typed and listed first. The choice is `remetered` in `edit/rewrite.ts`: only the `meter:` line the parser reads is rewritten (a trailing comment kept), or one is put under the header's last field line, or at the top where there is none. A commit of the editor, so Undo takes it back. Bars that no longer fit are the parser's to point out, and are: "1 to fix" and the bar marked, as for any bar that does not fit. Shown only with the pencil down, as the brief says.
- **4. Left: a style's page on a phone.** At 390 by 844 the strip ends at 142 px and JAZZ, at the size that fills the measure, is a 159 px box with 128 px of ink, so the name alone ends near 285 to 300 px; the two lines under it (what the style sounds like, its band and range) are 84 px more. Taking in the white space (about 45 px, worked out, not built) would bring the jazz page's first row from 443 to about 400, not under 300; it was not done, since it misses the mark and changes a passed look. Under 300 for every style would take one of: the two lines moved out of the head on a phone (under the list, or dropped), the strip set as one row that scrolls sideways (part 1 chose wrapped), or the name set smaller on a phone. Each is a design call for his eye.
- **5. A seat played on another of the style's instruments is exported with that instrument's General MIDI program.** `toMidi` takes the take's `instruments`; the program of a chosen pack is the instrument list's (`SOLO_INSTRUMENTS`, keyed by pack: the nylon-string guitar is 24, the steel-string 25), and the style's own number where the choice is the style's own pack. The app's worker and the CLI pass it, so smoke journey 5's bytes still agree. The soloist was already exported with the program of the instrument that played (from its notes' voice); checked, not changed. Track names name the seat ("Chords", "Solo") in every file, the style's own instrument included, so they were left: naming the instrument there would change every golden.
- **6. A kept take's Rename says the take's own name**: "Called “Kept take, 9:47 PM”" (the name the chart's More gives it) until it is named, and the box starts empty with that as its placeholder. A take is kept under its chart's name, which the app already reads as no name of its own (`takeName`, now in `takes.ts`); Rename was the one place that did not.
- **7. No change: a take or a recent entry whose chart was cut already behaves as asked.** Checked with a take naming `blues-numerals-bb` (cut), an old take naming no chart with the cut chart's text and title, and recents for the cut chart and that take: neither take's screen offers "Open its chart", the recent chart is not listed on home, and the take is listed, opens and plays from its own text. No cut chart's title is carried by a chart still in the library, so the old take's match by title finds nothing.
- **8. A chart's title on its screen runs to two lines, then is cut** (`.chart-title`, a two-line clamp). A title that fits on one line is the height it was (the line under it starts at 76 px, as before). The lists already wrap a title over as many lines as it needs and were left so; the More sheet's heading is still one line.
- **9. No change: the page already gives the dark paper to the dark theme** (`index.html` has had a `theme-color` for each scheme since the app went offline; Chrome in the dark scheme matches `#15132b`). The manifest's `theme_color` and `background_color` are the light paper, and a manifest cannot vary them by scheme; an installed app's splash and title bar before the page loads may be light. Not changed.
- **10. Left: the search box.** The first letter already keeps the box, its focus and the caret (one component at one place in the tree); what moves is where it is drawn, from under the name (78 px down at 390, at the left at 1280) to the library's pinned bar (2 px down, beside Home; at the right on a laptop). Keeping it under the finger would take the library showing what was found to keep home's top while the search came from home, with the way home somewhere else, or home's box moved to where the library's is: either reorganises the two screens.
- **`out/tidy-1/`** (the pictures of this work) is ignored by git.

## neo-soul in six and twelve (stage 6)

An Opus build from `prompts/meter-compound-style.md`, in its own clone on the branch `neosoul68`. Neo-soul's four seats carry `meters["12/8"]` and `meters["6/8"]`; `WRITTEN` in `test/takes.ts` has `neosoul: ["12/8", "6/8"]`; one chart in each meter. Nobody has heard it: the page for his ear is `out/meter-study-4-neosoul/` (not committed; `src/` has the script and the grid printer).

**What it was taken from.** Twelve has a tradition: the soul and gospel ballad in twelve-eight that neo-soul comes out of. It is played by neo-soul's own players: the loop, the lazy kick, the ghosts round the backbeat, the feathered hat, the deep late bass with rests, the held Rhodes, the guitar's late entries. Six has less of one (the slow jam in six), so it is the same players handed a bar of two. The rule that turns four into pulses is the one the notes give for every rule that says "beat" (AU-97), with one addition for neo-soul's sixteenths: a beat is a pulse, its "e" the triplet's middle eighth, its "and" and its "a" (the notes that lean into the next beat) the pulse's last eighth. The notes have no compound neo-soul beyond ST-neosoul-05 (the fit's rule).

**What each seat plays** (a pulse is three eighths, six sixteenths; only the eighths are struck but for one note, below):

- *Drums.* Quiet: the hat on each pulse and a ghost on its last eighth (`o...g.`), the section's kick, the backbeat (a cross-stick under 0.35), no ghosts. Working: the hat's triplets with the middle eighth ghosted (`x.g.o.`, four's `xgog`), the kick, the backbeat, one ghost set a section on the triplet's inner notes (three a bar in twelve, two in six), and in 35% of sections the late four on the second bar of each pair. Hot: the hot ghost sets (five in twelve, three in six), the expressive kicks, and on the second bar of a pair the hat breathing open on the last eighth of the second or the last pulse. Twelve's kicks are four's five with four's weights, each push on a pulse's last eighth (boom-bap, plain 1 and 3, a pickup into 3 into the "and" of 3, the stumble into 2, the Dilla skip); six's are the One, the One with a push into the next One, and a stumble into the backbeat. Fills: two eighths (a ghost with the hand on the hat, then the accent) or the last pulse (the snare on it, the mid tom accented on its last eighth), the lengths of four in eighths (phrase 0, 0, 4; section 4, 4, 6).
- *Bass.* Twelve plays four's five lines said in pulses, by four's weights per tier and section: the long root with the fifth into the One; two pulses of root, a breath, the fifth on the "a" of 3 and the seventh after 4; the lazy re-strike into 3; the hammer-on, root, octave on the "a" of 3 falling to the fifth; dead-note chatter. Six has five of its own, the late note after the backbeat or leaning into the One: `R----- ....5-`, `R---.. ..5-7-`, `R-..R- ----5-`, `R-2-R- ....5-`, `R-..m. ..R-7-`. The lead-in is the bassist's in every meter: the half step from below on the last sixteenth before a change.
- *Rhodes.* Twelve: four's figures said in pulses (quiet held, held with the "and" of 3, the "e" of 1 held; working the lazy re-strikes into 3 and into the One, the chord laid in on the "e" of 1; hot a re-strike leaning into every pulse). Six: held, leaning into the backbeat, or laid in late; working the One and late after the backbeat, the "e" of 1 leaning into the One, the lean into the backbeat; hot leaning into both pulses or late and leaning. Four's touch, holds, anticipation, and the hands closing under a soloist, unchanged.
- *Soloist.* Twelve: ST-neosoul-31's cells, endings and peak said in pulses, the levels and shapes four's (shared by name). Six: cells for a bar of two by the same late entries, three-quarters of four's notes a bar; endings land in the first pulse and hold through the second. Measured on the new charts with the soloist held at each level: 0.58, 0.88, 0.88 notes a second in twelve and 0.63, 0.79, 0.93 in six, against four's 0.70, 0.83 to 0.91, 1.01 to 1.44.

**Decisions the brief and the notes did not settle:**

- *The one note between the eighths is the bass's lead-in on the last sixteenth* (ST-neosoul-17, kept as the bassist's policy in every meter). It is neo-soul's own late push, the one sixteenth four has that is not on its grid's eighths, and a property says where it may be: a sixteenth before a chord it leads into. Everything else is on the eighths (the every-style drum property demands it of the time, and the notes have no sixteenth for a compound neo-soul).
- *The late four moves to the triplet's middle eighth*, one step of the grid the band plays, as it is a sixteenth in four (ST-neosoul-08: a written rhythm). It is twice as late in time (an eighth at 58 is 0.34 s against a sixteenth's 0.18 s at 85); his ear decides.
- *No Dilla skip in six*: three kicks and the backbeat would be four of six eighths (AU-96).
- *Six's hat and backbeat are twelve's first two pulses* (`half` in `drums.ts`); everything else in six is written for six.
- *Tempo: the default two thirds in both, 47 to 67, usually 57; nothing in `tempo.meters`.* It keeps the eighths at four's speed, and a soul ballad in twelve sits in that range (a bar 3.6 to 5.1 s). The charts are at 58 and 60.
- *The hot hammer-on in six leaves four's octave out*: with it and the pickup, the bar had six notes in six eighths, and under a slash chord the 2nd below a low bass note leapt fourteen semitones to the octave (found by the full run).
- *The charts.* `neosoul-ballad-twelve-db.chart`, "Soul Ballad in Twelve": Db, 12/8 at 58, sixteen bars, a verse `Dbmaj9 | Bbm9 | Ebm9 Ab13 | Dbmaj9 Gb13 | Fm7 | Bbm9 | Ebm9 | Ab7sus4 Ab13` and a chorus `Gbmaj9 | Fm9 | Ebm9 | Dbmaj9 | Gbmaj9 | Gbm9 | Fm7 Bb7b9 | Ebm9 Ab13`. `neosoul-slow-six-fm.chart`, "Slow Jam in Six": F minor, 6/8 at 60, AABA of thirty-two, the A `Fm9 | Dbmaj9 | Bbm9 | C7#9 | Fm9 | Dbmaj9 | Gm7b5 C7b9 | Fm9`, the bridge `Abmaj9 | Gm7b5 C7b9 | Fm9 | Ebm9 Ab13 | Dbmaj9 | Bbm9 Eb13 | Abmaj9 | Gm7b5 C7b9`.

**One engine change, in the fit: a fitted bar of dotted pulses keeps its kick and snare to half its eighths** (`groove.ts`, `notBusy`). Rock, fitted into six on the new chart in six, put four's "and" of 1 (its fifth kick), an answer bar's kick into the One and the backbeat in one bar: four of six eighths, against the every-style property (AU-96, EAR-11). Where a fitted bar is busier than half, the kick's strokes off the pulses go, earliest first, so the turn is kept. A bar within half is untouched: over every compound chart, every style, every kind on six seeds and held at three levels, the only takes that moved were the eight rock takes that failed. No golden or scorecard reads a fitted compound bar.

**Properties.** Neo-soul had none of its own; adding it to `WRITTEN` holds it in six and twelve to the every-style rules that hold only where a style plays its own parts (the bass's octave leap, its last note, a note outside the scale only passing, a section keeping its figure). Added, each run against the fit first:

- *the loop's time*, in four, six and twelve: the kick on the One, a backbeat on every pulse between the strong ones, the last of them possibly one grid step late in a second bar. **Passed against the fit.**
- *the hat's triplets*: from the working level every eighth and a ghost in every bar; quiet the pulse and at most its last eighth, never the middle, no ghost. **Failed against the fit** (no hat on the second eighth).
- *the whole band on the eighths*, fills and soloist too, nothing swinging them, the one exception the bass's sixteenth pickup before a chord. **Failed against the fit** (a 4/4 fill's snare between the eighths).
- *the bass's line*: in a bar of one chord, the One struck, a note between the pulses, never every pulse. **Failed against the fit** (only the pulses).
- *the Rhodes holds and leans*: away from a soloist, never every pulse, and from the working level a strike between the pulses. **Failed against the fit** (a chord on every pulse).

**Nothing that exists changed.** The goldens pass byte for byte. The eight scorecards, rewritten to a scratch file on their own charts (neo-soul on its five named one by one), are byte for byte their baselines, with the parts in and again after the fit change. Neo-soul's is accepted with the new charts (`score --style neosoul --update`).

**The new charts alone, as neo-soul** (200 takes each). Twelve: the kick on the One and the bass on the One 100%, ghosts 4.03 a bar, bass silence 27%, register 35.3, approaches from below 80%, colour 91%, the soloist's changes, beats and phrase ends on chord tones 100%, 98%, 100%, empty solo bars 6%. Six: the same 100%s, ghosts 2.29 a bar (20% of takes inside 2.5 to 5, a band measured on bars of four: six's bar is half as long), bass silence 27%, register 34.4, approaches from below 71%, colour 90%, empty solo bars 25%. Outside on both: `bass.syncopated-sixteenths` 12% and 9% against 25% to 60% (it counts the sixteenths between the eighths, which in six and twelve are only the pickup: by design); `chords.lowest-note` 54.9 and 55.0 with 77% and 78% inside; `solo.builds` 79% inside in twelve.

**Found, not fixed:**

- *Jazz's fitted soloist on the chart in six, as jazz's own* (the probe; not in the suite, where jazz is handed it once): a chromatic note left hanging (E over Fm9, the bar's last eighth, the next note an eighth after the barline; seed 2, 160) and F# held or on a pulse over Fm9 (song with solo, seed 1, bar 19). Jazz in six is the fit until jazz writes it.
- *The bass in six is busy at the hot level for neo-soul*: 4.6 notes a bar of six eighths with its pickups (2.7 quiet, 3.3 working).
- The scorecard's drum and comping measures for the backbeat, the late four and held chords read bars of four and say nothing of six and twelve.

**Every style on every chart.** `pnpm test:full` passes (it failed once on this branch before the fit change above, rock on the chart in six, and once on the hammer-on's leap, both fixed). Each of the eight styles played each new chart as its own (the rest of the library out of the way, every kind on six seeds, the soloist's kinds): on the chart in twelve nothing failed but the floors a one-chart library cannot meet; on the chart in six, the same, and jazz's two soloist findings above.

**Timings** (the machine shared with other builders, each run alone and beside the commit this branch started from): neo-soul's own tests alone 7.5 and 7.6 s (the start, 6.2 and 6.0; 6.7 at a load of 4 before any change); `pnpm check` 18.6 and 18.9 s of 20 (the start, 18.8 and 19.1 beside it); `pnpm test:full` 47.4 s of 60; `pnpm score` 60.9 s of 120, no movement in any of the eight; `pnpm smoke` 12.4 s of 60.

On the main thread: the one engine change read (`notBusy`: a fitted bar of dotted pulses drops kick strokes off the pulses until kick and snare are on no more than half its eighths; the builder compared every style on every compound chart before and after and only the eight failing rock takes moved), the two charts read (`neosoul-ballad-twelve-db`, `neosoul-slow-six-fm`), no golden in the diff, neo-soul's scorecard the only one rewritten. - **Left by the builder**: jazz's soloist, still the fit in 6/8, left a chromatic note hanging and played an F sharp over F minor nine on the new chart in six under the strongest probe (not in the suite's material): for the build that writes jazz in 6/8.

## neo-soul in six and twelve, heard; the late four is a sixteenth late there too (stage 6)

Brandon, 2026-11-16, after `out/meter-study-4-neosoul/`: "it might drag a little - i trust your judgment. overall it grooves pretty well though, and i really dig what you did". The feels stand.

- **The late four in six and twelve is the backbeat a sixteenth after its pulse**, where it was written on the triplet's middle eighth: 0.17 s late at 58, as it is in four (0.18 s at 85), not 0.34 s. Twice as late in time is the likeliest thing to have dragged, and it is the one place the build chose a grid step where four's is a length of time. Three rules are restated to say so: neo-soul's loop (the last backbeat may be a sixteenth late in any meter), neo-soul's band on the eighths (the late four is the second note allowed between them), and the every-style rule that a drummer's time in dotted pulses is on the eighths (a backbeat a style writes late is the snare a sixteenth after a pulse, nowhere else). Neo-soul's scorecard re-accepted; no golden moved. **Not heard since the change.**
## funk in six and twelve (stage 6, during gate 6)

An Opus build from `prompts/meter-compound-style.md` in its own clone on the branch `funk68`. Funk's four seats carry `meters["12/8"]` and `meters["6/8"]`; `WRITTEN` in `test/takes.ts` has `funk: ["6/8", "12/8"]`; one chart in each meter. Nobody has heard it: the page for his ear is `out/meter-study-4-funk/` (not committed; `src/` has the script and the grid printer).

**Which it is: no tradition, a funk band handed the chart.** Funk has no book of its own in compound time. The nearest thing players reach for, the half-time shuffle (the Purdie and Porcaro grooves), is a bar of four with a triplet under it and its backbeat on 3 alone; written as a bar of 12/8 it would put one backbeat in a bar of four slow pulses, and it belongs to a chart in four with a shuffle, which the format already writes. So this is what funk's players do with a chart in twelve or six, taken from funk's own 4/4 parts (ST-funk-06 to ST-funk-33) said in pulses as the design says (AU-97): a beat is a pulse; an "and" or an "a", the push into the next beat, is the pulse's last eighth; an "e" is its middle eighth. Nothing swings and nothing is struck between the eighths: funk's feel lives in its sixteenths, and a bar of dotted pulses has none to put it on, so the brief's exception is not taken (the property "funk: in six and twelve the eighths are the band's smallest note" says so).

**What each seat plays** (the grids are in the page; counts are over six seeds of each new chart held at a level, three passes):

- *Drums, twelve.* The kick has the One, never 2 or 4, and an eighth off the pulse in every bar: five kicks of three strokes, one a section, the busier forward as the band is louder (1, the "and" of 2, the middle of 3; 1 and its middle, the "and" of 3; 1 and the "and"s of 2 and 3; 1 and its "and", the middle of 4; 1, the middle of 2, the "and" of 3). The backbeat on 2 and 4 at every level. Quiet: the hat on each pulse and its last eighth (`x...o.`, four's `x.o.` in pulses), no ghosts, 7.7 hat strokes and 3.5 kicks a bar. From the working level the hat on all twelve eighths, the pulse and its last eighth leaned on and the middle soft (four's `xoxo`), ghosts round the backbeat off the pulses (one of four sets a section, 3.4 a bar); the hat opens on the bar's last eighth into the One in 35% of sections at the working level and in every bar hot. The answer bar (2, 4, 6 of a phrase) turns its back half: quiet a soft kick into 4 or the One; louder a kick into 4 or the One, ghosts on the middle and last eighths of 4, or the open hat, in four's order and weights. Fills are four's shapes in eighths, ending on the last eighth, the loudest last, never the toms: the last eighth alone (a snare under the hat, the band hit, the hat barking), or a pulse (the ghost drag, the hit, kick and snare, the hat cut, a snare build); phrase fills an eighth (a pulse hot), section fills a pulse.
- *Drums, six.* The hat and the backbeat are twelve's first half (`halfOf` in `drums.ts`): the backbeat on the second pulse (SU-D-14: a bar of two has no beat 3). The kicks are two strokes, the One and an eighth off the pulse (the push into the One; the One's middle; its "and" into the backbeat; the middle of the second pulse); quiet only the two whose second stroke is in the back half. In six a turn cannot add a kick (two kicks and the backbeat are already half the bar's six eighths), so quiet the turn moves the back-half kick to its other eighth, and louder it is the hands' (ghosts on the second pulse's last two eighths, or the open hat). Fills an eighth, or the bar's last two eighths (a pulse would take the backbeat: it is half the bar). Quiet 3.9 hat strokes and 2.0 kicks a bar; working 5.4 hat, 2.2 ghosts.
- *Bass.* One riff a section, locked to the kick (`followsKick`, a root under every kick the riff does not cover), short (three quarters of the gap, at most two and a quarter sixteenths), on the eighths. Twelve: four's five riffs said in pulses, turning over all four (`R...m. R...8. m...R. ..7.8.` and four more), drawn under four's name so a section's riff is the same one in both meters. Six: five riffs of one strong pulse and one answer, each ending on a note on the last eighth that pushes into the One (`R.m... R...8.`, `R..... m.R.5.`, `R.5.m. ..7.8.`, `R...8. m...R.`, `R.8... ....R.`), written, not halves of twelve's. Quiet the roots (3.9 notes a bar in twelve, 2.5 in six); working without the dead notes on a pulse's last eighth (7.3; 4.3); hot all of it (7.7; 4.3). Turns at a phrase's end: the pickup (the fifth and the seventh into the One) and, hot, the octaves.
- *Guitar.* The hand keeps swinging in sixteenths, so every stroke on an eighth is a downstroke and the upstrokes between are unheard; at the meter's usual tempo that hand moves at four's speed. Three lines a section: the backbeat (a stab on the pulse's last eighth into the backbeat, the backbeat leaned on, its own last eighth), two against three (a stab every two eighths, three across each two pulses: compound time's own cross-rhythm where four has three against four), and the ands. Quiet the stabs alone (6.2 strokes a bar in twelve, 3.2 in six); working one scratch a pulse, the last the line leaves open in it (10.0; 5.1); hot every eighth (11.9; 6.0). Twelve's backbeat and cross-rhythm lines are six's twice (`twiceOver`); the ands are written for each. A phrase's last bar stabs once more in its last pulse, as four's flick does. Under a soloist the line stands (CH-68), as in four.
- *Alto.* Four's cells said in pulses, clipped (`x.` is still an eighth let go after a sixteenth), on the eighths only; the same three levels by name and four's phrase shapes (now one table, `SHAPES`, read by every meter); no pickups and nothing over the barline (ST-funk-33). A bar of twelve is half as long again as a bar of four at the usual tempo, so its levels ask 3, 5 and 8 notes a bar; six's 1.5, 2.6 and 4.1. Endings land on the phrase's long note and leave the last pulse unstruck.

**One engine change: a lead-in "a sixteenth before" is the eighth before, in a bar of dotted pulses the style has written its line for** (`leadInIn` in `players/bass/play.ts`). Funk's lead-in sits on the last sixteenth (ST-funk-21), and the engine's step into the next root (BA-07) was adding a note there in a bar of twelve, between the eighths (found by the bass property on the slow dance, quiet: G1 on the last sixteenth before F7). Where the line is written for the meter the last eighth is its last place, so funk's half step under the next root now comes there, 30% of the time from the working level as in four. It applies only to a style that has written lines for a compound meter and leads in on the sixteenth: funk here. Neo-soul also leads in on the sixteenth; once its compound lines are merged this rule applies to it too, which its builder should know.

**Properties.** Restated in pulses and held in four, six and twelve (`funkPlayed`: funk's own takes, and each library chart in a meter held at each level): *the kick is on the One and stays off the backbeat, the snare cracks the backbeat* (2 and 4; in six the second pulse; a fill has the last); *the beat is two bars long* (the back half is the bar's second half); *the bass is on the One and under the kick* (on the eighths in a bar of dotted pulses); *the riff is the section's* (the bar's first half). Added: *funk: in six and twelve the hat keeps the eighths and the kick is off the pulse*; *the bass plays the section's riff, not the kick's roots* (from the working level more than roots before the last eighth, which may lead in; quiet roots alone); *the guitar scratches the eighths* (quiet no scratch, working more strokes than pulses and a scratch, hot every eighth but where a leant chord rings); *the eighths are the band's smallest note*, nothing between them and nothing moved by swing. **Against the fit** (the library's compound charts, before any data): every added property failed (a bar of six whose only kick was the One; the bass's roots alone; two strokes on the guitar in a bar of six; a 4/4 fill's snare between the eighths), and so did two restated ones: the two-bar beat (the fit's answer bar in six was the bar before again) and the bass on the grid (in a quiet bar of the slow dance the bass's step into the next root on the last sixteenth, the fault the engine change above mends for a written bar; under the fit it stays). The kick and backbeat rule and the riff's first half passed under the fit.

**The guitar-in-six rules are acoustic's.** "A guitar picks a bar of six as six-eight is picked" and "a guitar strums a bar of six as a pendulum in eighths" were held by every guitar that has written six-eight; funk's hand neither picks nor swings in eighths. They now hold the guitars whose hand swings in eighths (`guitarsInSix` in `test/piano.test.ts`); acoustic is the only one, so nothing they held moves.

**Decided here, because the brief and the notes did not settle it:**

- *Tempo: the default, two thirds of four's, in both meters: 53 to 84, usually 70.* It keeps the eighths at four's eighth-note speed and the guitar's sixteenth hand at four's; nothing is written in `tempo.meters`. The charts are at 70 (twelve) and 74 (six).
- *The kick and the snare are held to half the eighths by the writing*: three-stroke kicks in twelve, so an answer bar's added kick makes six of twelve with the backbeat; two-stroke kicks in six and no added kick there. Four's fifth kick (four strokes) has no counterpart.
- *Six's fills stop short of the backbeat* (four sixteenths at most, the bar's last two eighths): "a beat at most" (ST-funk-10) would be half the bar.
- *The bass's pickup turn in six takes its fifth over the root*, not under as four's and twelve's do: in six the turn is half the bar and follows the riff's first pulse, which may end on its pop, and under the root the line fell 17 semitones (found by the full run).
- *Six's ghost sets never hold both of the second pulse's off eighths*, so that a turn of ghosts there is always heard.
- *The guitar's working level is one scratch a pulse*: four keeps the scratches on its eighths, and a pulse of three eighths has no such subset; a scratch on the One gives way to the chord's own stab where it arrives, so the last scratch of each pulse is the one kept.
- *The charts.* `library/funk-twelve-eight-gm.chart`, "Minor Funk in Twelve", G minor, 12/8 at 70, thirty-two bars AABA: the A `Gm9 | % | C9 | % | Gm9 | % | Eb9 D7#9 | Gm9` (the minor one and its dorian four, home from the flat six through the five a half bar each); the bridge `Ebmaj7 | D7#9 | Gm9 | C9 | Ebmaj7 | F9 | D9sus4 | D7#9`. `library/funk-six-eight-bb.chart`, "Funk in Six", B-flat, 6/8 at 74: a sixteen-bar verse `Bb9 | % | % | % | Eb9 | % | Bb9 | % | Cm7 | F9 | Bb9 | G7#9 | Cm7 | F9 | Bb9 | %` and an eight-bar chorus `Eb9 | % | Bb9 | % | Eb9 | % | Cm7 F9 | Bb9 F9`, its last two bars a chord a pulse. Funk's library is in C, Am, E, Dm and A; these are in a horn key each. No named tune.

**Not built, and left:** funk's half-time shuffle (above), as a chart in four would want it; anything between the eighths (a sextuplet scratch hot would be the funk hand's own sixteenths, and nobody has asked); a lead-in policy of its own for a meter (the bass carries lines for a meter and nothing else; the one rule above was enough).

**Nothing that exists changed.** Every golden take passes byte for byte. The eight scorecards, rewritten to a scratch file on their own charts after the engine change and before the charts were added, are byte for byte their baselines, and the seven that are not funk's are again after the charts went in. Funk's is accepted with the two new charts (`score --style funk --update`); `pnpm score` reports no movement (63.6 s of 120, the machine shared).

**The new charts alone, as funk** (200 takes each). *Minor Funk in Twelve*: the kick and the bass on the One, the changes stated, every kick under a bass note, ghosts in every bar, the answer bars and their turns held, each 94 to 100%; dead notes 16% of the bass; the guitar's grip 57.6 low, its top moving 3.2 semitones a change (all in band). Outside their bands: `bass.syncopated-sixteenths` 0% (target 10% to 60%: it counts sixteenths, and in twelve the riff's syncopations are on the eighths, so it says nothing of it); `bass.half-step-approaches` 34%, 48% of takes under the 35% ceiling (the chart's own roots move by half step, Eb to D twice in the A, and the lead-in now has its eighth); `solo.cost` 2.88 against 2.4 (no take inside), `solo.builds` 1.30 (15% inside), `solo.empty-bars` 2% (21% inside): the slow-bar reading rock's and the blues' charts in twelve showed (a bar at 70 is 3.4 s, longer than the soloist's slowest bar of 3.2), not told apart from the meter. *Funk in Six*: the same drum, bass and guitar measures in band, `bass.half-step-approaches` 20%; `bass.syncopated-sixteenths` 0% for the same reason; `solo.short-notes` 80% (48% of takes over the 80% floor), `solo.repeated-notes` 73% inside, `solo.builds` 1.52 (38% inside), `solo.empty-bars` 25% (in band).

**Every style on every chart.** `pnpm test:full` passes with the two charts in the library (55.5 s of 60 under load 9; the start commit 52.2 s beside it). Each of the eight styles played the two charts as if they were its own (every kind of take on six seeds and the soloist's kinds, the rest of the library out of the way): beyond the floors on how many cases a property met, which a two-chart library cannot meet, three styles fail, none of them changed by this branch (none writes a compound line, and the engine change reads only a style that does):

- *A major seventh over the dominant tonic*, "the soloist plays no wrong note on a beat or held": acoustic on the six, seed 1, a jam of four passes with the soloist, bar 72, A4 over Bb9; reggae on the six, seed 3, the same kind, bar 48, A4 over Bb9; neo-soul on the twelve, seed 1, the same kind, bar 39, D5 over Eb9. The key's seventh played over a chord whose seventh is flat: a I9 is funk's usual tonic, and these soloists' key notes do not allow for it. Not checked in four; not fixed (it is three styles' note pools).
- *Reggae's solo on the six, seed 1, from bar 25: 3.0 bars of silence*, "a solo breathes and keeps going". A bar of six at 74 is 1.6 s, so three bars are 4.9 s. The fitted soloist in a short bar; not fixed.

**Timings** (the machine shared with other builders; each beside the commit this branch started from). Funk's own tests alone (`ENSEMBLE_STYLES=funk node --test --test-isolation=none test/*.test.ts`): 8.0 s at the start, 10.4 s on the branch (load 2), under the 14. The push suite: 20.3 s at the start and 21.1 s here under load 10 to 12; funk is now its slowest style, and on a quiet machine the start's 17.8 should be about 18.8, inside 20 with a second to spare. `pnpm smoke` 10.9 s of 60.

## funk in six and twelve: checked and merged (stage 6)

An Opus build from `prompts/meter-compound-style.md`, merged over neo-soul's. On the main thread: the engine change read and narrowed, the two charts read (`funk-twelve-eight-gm`, `funk-six-eight-bb`), no golden in the diff, funk's scorecard the only one rewritten by the branch. The full suite passes untimed on the merged tree (two builds were running); `pnpm score` no movement in any of the eight. 62 charts.

- **The lead-in's place in a bar of dotted pulses is the style's to say** (`LeadInPolicy.inDotted`). The builder had moved every sixteenth lead-in to the eighth before wherever a style writes its compound line, which was right for funk (its line there is all eighths) and would have taken away neo-soul's lazy sixteenth pickup, which is written, held by a property and heard by Brandon. Funk says `inDotted: "eighth"`; neo-soul says nothing and keeps its sixteenth.
- **Funk in compound time is funk's 4/4 said in pulses**, not the half-time shuffle (one backbeat in four slow pulses): the builder's choice, for his ear.
- **Found in other styles on funk's charts under the strongest probe, not in the suite's material, left**: a soloist's major seventh over a dominant tonic (acoustic and reggae on the chart in six, neo-soul on the chart in twelve; not checked in 4/4), and reggae's soloist silent for three bars on the chart in six.
- Funk's own tests alone take about 10 to 13 s; the push suite's timing waits for a quiet machine.
## disco in six and twelve (stage 6)

An Opus build from `prompts/meter-compound-style.md`, in its own clone on the branch `disco68`. Disco's four seats carry `meters["12/8"]` and `meters["6/8"]`; `WRITTEN` in `test/takes.ts` has `disco: ["12/8", "6/8"]`; one chart in each meter. Nobody has heard it: the page for his ear is `out/meter-study-4-disco/` (not committed; `src/` has the script and the grid printer).

**What it was taken from.** Disco has no tradition in compound time that the notes or this build can cite, so it is disco's players handed a bar of pulses of three eighths: the floor on every pulse, the backbeat on the pulses between the strong ones, the open hat, the octave pump and the guitar's chop on each pulse's "and". That is ST-disco-03, the previous version's rule for any other meter, written out as the style's own parts. Four is turned into pulses by AU-97 (a beat is a pulse, its "and" the pulse's last eighth), with one rule for what four puts on its "e" and "a": the triplet's middle eighth, the one place left between the pulse and the "and" (the shaker, the closed hat's second stroke, the guitar's light lean into the chop). Nothing is between the eighths, nothing swings.

**What each seat plays** (a pulse is three eighths, six sixteenths; only the eighths are struck):

- *Drums.* Every level: the kick on every pulse, through every fill (no fill writes a kick). Quiet: the kick soft, the closed hat on each pulse and a ghost on its last eighth (four's `o.g.`), the backbeat soft, a cross-stick under 0.30. Working: the open hat on each pulse's last eighth, the backbeat accented, and the section's hat: the skeleton (the chick on the pulse, the shaker on the middle eighth) one time in three, or the triplet (closed on the pulse and the middle eighth, the next pulse choking the open hat). Hot: the triplet with the shaker, and on every second bar the lift, the bar's last open hat accented with the snare on the same eighth. Fills, four's lengths said in pulses (its two sixteenths the last eighth, its beat a pulse, its two beats two pulses): the bark (the open hat and the snare together on the last eighth; quiet, the closed hat), a pulse on the snare crescendo, the disco roll in two pulses (the long-short, then the triplet), and hot the Philly fill down the kit an eighth a drum (a pulse: snare, mid tom, floor; two pulses: snare twice, high, mid twice, floor). Six's floor, bark, backbeat and hats are twelve's first two pulses (`half`); its lift and its fills (none longer than a pulse) are its own. Measured over four seeds held at a level on the new charts: twelve 13.9, 18.0, 22.2 strokes a bar quiet, working, hot (the fit 14.3, 14.5, 14.7), the kick and snare on 4.3 to 4.6 of twelve eighths; six 6.9, 9.0, 11.3 (the fit 7.2 to 7.6), on 2.2 to 2.4 of six.
- *Bass.* Twelve, four's figures said in pulses with four's weights: quiet the root on every pulse (3), or with the octave as a pickup on the bar's last eighth (2); working the pump, root on the pulse and octave on its last eighth (4), the octave only on 2 and 4 (1), the pump in the back half (1); hot the pump (2) or the triplet pump, root, root, octave (2). Six: quiet the same two; working the pump (4) or the octave on the backbeat only (2, four's two back-half figures being one in a bar of two); hot the same two. The lead-in is four's: the half step below the next root, on the last note within an eighth of the change. 4.4, 8.0, 10.0 notes a bar in twelve; 2.4, 4.0, 5.0 in six.
- *Guitar.* The hand still swings in sixteenths, down on every eighth and up between unheard, so in a bar of pulses it touches the strings on downstrokes only. Twelve: quiet a scratch on the pulse and the chop on its last eighth; working the "and"s (every eighth, the chop on the last) or the Nile line (an accented chop on the One, the chops on the "and"s of 2 and 4 with a light stroke on the middle eighth before each); hot the "and"s (1), the pickup (a light stroke leaning into the chops of 1 and 3, 1) and the push (into every chop, 2). Six: the same figures for a bar of two. Under a soloist the hand keeps its figure, as in four (CH-68: disco's guitar does not listen).
- *Alto.* Twelve: ST-disco-23's cells, endings and peak said in pulses, a sixteenth burst of three or four the triplet; levels and shapes four's. Six: cells for a bar of two from the same material, endings landing in the first pulse and holding through the second. Measured with the band held: 2.7, 3.7, 4.3 notes a bar of twelve and 1.6, 1.6, 2.2 of six.

**Decisions the brief and the notes did not settle:**

- *Tempo: four's range in both meters, 100 to 136, usually 120 dotted quarters* (`tempo.meters`), not the default two thirds (67 to 91). Disco's figure is the floor's: the kick on every pulse at the dancer's speed. At the same figure a bar of twelve is as long as a bar of four, and the pump, the bark, the chop and the kick come as often; the eighths are a triplet at six a second against four's sixteenths at eight. Two thirds would put the floor at a slow jam's pace. His ear decides; it is one line to change.
- *Nothing between the eighths, the guitar's hand included.* Disco's feel lives in a sixteenth hand, and at 120 dotted quarters sixteenths would run at four's speed; but the drummer's time is held to the eighths by an every-style property and each style written so far kept to them. The hand swings in sixteenths and strikes the downstrokes.
- *The hot gallop is not played*: its short notes are sixteenths and its "a" falls on the "and". Hot takes the triplet pump instead, a note on every eighth (twelve a bar, the gallop's count). The busiest bass of any style in compound time; his ear decides.
- *The lift's snare is on the bar's last eighth with the accented open hat*: four's "a" of 4 is that eighth in pulses.
- *The bark as a fill is one eighth long*, the open hat and the snare together, so the open hat stays on its "and".
- *Fill lengths*: twelve phrase 2, 2, 6 and section 6, 12, 12 sixteenths (quiet, working, hot); six phrase 2, 2, 6 and section 6, 6, 6.
- *The lift comes every second bar in six too*, every four pulses: six's two bars are twelve's one, and the loop's turn is the bar pair's.
- *A level's notes a bar are two thirds of four's in both meters* (1.2, 2.4, 4): the critic counts them for a bar of four quarters, and at disco's figure a bar of twelve lasts what a bar of four does and a bar of six half of it, so each is asked four's notes a pulse.
- *Six's alto never lands on the first pulse's last eighth alone*: at 136 the last pulse of a bar of six is 0.44 s, less than disco's breath, and the phrase ran into the next (found by the full run).
- *The charts.* `disco-shuffle-twelve-gm.chart`, "Disco Shuffle in Twelve": G minor, 12/8 at 112, a verse `Gm9 | Cm9 | Gm9 | Cm9 | Ebmaj7 | Am7b5 D7b9 | Gm9 | %` and a chorus `Ebmaj7 | F9 | Dm7 | Gm9 | Cm9 | F9 | Bbmaj7 Ebmaj7 | Am7b5 D7b9`. `disco-floor-six-d.chart`, "Disco in Six": D, 6/8 at 116, thirty-two bars: a verse `Dmaj9 | % | Em7 | A13 | Dmaj9 | % | Bm9 | Em7 A13`, a second verse `Dmaj9 | % | Em7 | A13 | F#m7 | B7 | Em7 | A13`, a chorus `Gmaj9 | % | F#m7 | Bm9 | Em7 | % | A9sus4 | A13 | Gmaj9 | % | F#m7 | B7 | Em7 | A13 | Dmaj9 | A13`. A bar of six at 116 is a second, so most chords last two. Em7, not Em9: disco's guitar voices a written m9 with the 9th on top, and over this chart the hand had to shift more than four frets at 15% of the changes (`chords.neck-jumps`, in four as in six); with Em7 none.

**One engine change, for acoustic, found on the chart in six: an upstroke leaves no growl on the bottom of what it catches** (`guitar.ts`). Acoustic voices A13 root, 3rd, 13th, 7th, and an upstroke catching the top three strings sounded F#3 G3 C#4, a half step on the bottom under E4, against "the hand does not growl" (in four too: a chart of A13s in D gave 4 to 7 such strokes in 42). The stroke now catches strings downward until its bottom is clean where the grip's was. No golden moves; every scorecard is byte for byte.

**The tests play a borrowed chart at the borrower's tempo in two more places** (`test/takes.ts`): the soloist's kinds that name no tempo, and the takes held at a level (`heldTakes`). Both played another style's chart at its own figure, which the app never does (`tempoFor`), and neo-soul met the new charts at 112 and 116 dotted quarters, twice the top of its range: its bass's lean came to 47 and 49 ticks against the 40 the band's on-the-eighths property allows, and three of seven phrases ran together. At 57 neither happens.

**Properties.** Restated: *four on the floor* in pulses (the kick on every pulse of every bar at every level through every fill; from the working level the open hat on every "and", each pulse's last eighth in six and twelve; quiet, never open), held in four, six and twelve. **Passed against the fit** (the fit's floor is every beat's kick, and its open hat already falls on a pulse's last eighth). Adding disco to `WRITTEN` holds it in six and twelve to the every-style rules for a style's own parts (the bass's octave leap, last note and passing notes, a section keeping its figure, the strumming pendulum); all passed against the fit. Added, each run against the fit first:

- *the hat is the triplet*: from the working level every eighth has the hat or the shaker; quiet, the closed hat alone on the pulse and its last eighth, never the middle. **Failed against the fit** (nothing on the second eighth).
- *the bass pumps with the floor*: every note on an eighth, every pulse struck; quiet nothing between the pulses but a pickup on the bar's last eighth; from the working level a pulse's last eighth struck in every bar, the octave over the pulse heard; below hot never the middle eighth. **Failed against the fit** (only the pulses).
- *the guitar's chop is on the "and"*: a chord sounding on the last eighth of each backbeat pulse in every bar, soloist or not; nothing between the eighths; quiet only the pulses and their last eighths; from the working level every eighth touched (the One may be a chord leant into, ringing). **Failed against the fit** (no chop on the "and").
- *the whole band on the eighths*, fills and soloist too, nothing swinging them. **Failed against the fit** (a 4/4 fill's snare on a sixteenth).
- *Restated, not disco's*: the acoustic rules for a guitar in six ("picks a bar of six as six-eight is picked", "strums a bar of six as a pendulum in eighths" and three floors) asked it of every guitar that has written six, so disco's chuck was held to acoustic's picking. They are now asked of a guitar that strums eighths and has written six.

**Nothing that exists changed.** The goldens pass byte for byte. The eight scorecards, rewritten to a scratch file on their own charts (disco on its four named one by one), are byte for byte their baselines with the parts and tempo in and the charts out, and again after the engine change with the charts in. Disco's is accepted with the new charts (`score --style disco --update`).

**The new charts alone, as disco** (200 takes each). Twelve: the kick on the One, the bass on the One, the changes stated and the kicks doubled 100%; the bass's register 37.2 (96% inside), its notes 1.7 sixteenths long; the guitar's lowest note 62.5, no voicing faults, no neck jumps; the soloist's changes on chord tones 100%, beats on chord tones 97%, empty bars 17%, 1.35 notes a second in its first chorus and 2.50 in its last (four's 1.10 and 2.77), every phrase breathing. Six: the same 100%s; register 36.7 (97%); lowest note 62.7; empty solo bars 22%, 1.31 and 2.30 notes a second. Outside their bands: `solo.short-notes` 58% and 56% against 60% to 90% (the share of notes an eighth or shorter: where four's cells run in sixteenths, the pulses' cells hold a note two eighths, `x---x-`), `solo.builds` 64% and 83% of takes inside, `solo.rises` 79% and 92%, `solo.bars-that-rhyme` 94% (twelve), `solo.high-points-deserved` 91% and `solo.silent-sixteenths` 83% (six). The drum measures for the backbeat and the cymbal under it, and the comping measures for strokes with the drums, read bars of four and say nothing of six and twelve.

**Found, not fixed:**

- *A bar of six at disco's figure is one second, which the soloist reads as a fast bar* (`slowBar` 3.2, `fastBar` 1.8 are seconds a bar): in six it always plays a level sparser than its arc asks. The reading is the engine's and counts bars, not pulses.
- *The new chart in six puts the disco guitar's Em9 a hand's shift from its neighbours*: written as Em7 here. A minor ninth on disco's three-string grip has its 9th on top, high over the aim (ST-disco-16); older than this work.
- The scorecard's drum and comping measures for the backbeat and the strokes read bars of four only.

**Every style on every chart.** `pnpm test:full` passes (it failed on this branch before the two harness lines and the upstroke above, and once on disco's own ending in six). Each of the eight styles played each new chart as its own (the rest of the library out of the way, the chart's tempo held inside the style's range, every kind on six seeds, the soloist's kinds): nothing failed but the floors a one-chart library cannot meet.

**Timings** (the machine shared with other builders; each run alone and beside the commit this branch started from): disco's own tests alone 10.4 and 10.9 s (the start, 9.5 and 8.9 beside it; 8.8 and 8.6 on a quiet machine before any change), under 14; `pnpm test` 21.6 and 19.9 s of 20 at a load of 8 to 12 (the start, 19.2 and 18.5 beside it): **about a second and a half more than the start, at or over the budget under load.** The new charts are held at three levels by every style that writes their meter (rock and acoustic one more chart each, neo-soul two), so they cost the suite in other styles' processes as well as disco's (rock alone 9.1 to 10.1 s, neo-soul 9.5 to 10.1). Not consolidated here; the held takes on a push are where to take it back. `pnpm test:full` 51.2 s of 60 (the start, 52.5 beside it); `pnpm score` 66.2 s of 120, no movement in any of the eight; `pnpm smoke` 11.2 s of 60.

## disco in six and twelve: checked and merged (stage 6)

An Opus build from `prompts/meter-compound-style.md`, merged over funk's. On the main thread: the two charts read (`disco-shuffle-twelve-gm`, `disco-floor-six-d`), no golden in the diff, disco's scorecard the only one rewritten by the branch; the merge kept both builders' test changes. The full suite passes untimed on the merged tree; `pnpm score` no movement in any of the eight. 64 charts.

- **Two rules restated at the merge**, each surfaced by the new charts moving which takes a style is handed:
  - *A solo's longest silence is three bars, and in six-eight three bars of four pulses.* Disco's soloist on a chart in six left "3.0 bars of silence" that is three seconds long: at 120 a bar of six is one second. The rule was written when a bar was four beats; in a bar of two dotted pulses it now counts the same stretch of time.
  - *Jazz's rule that a note from outside the scale goes somewhere is held where jazz has written its soloist* (4/4, 3/4, 6/4), not in six-eight or twelve-eight, where jazz's line is still the fit's and left a chromatic note hanging on neo-soul's chart in six. It comes back when jazz is written there.
- **Held takes are on one seed on a push and two on the full run.** Every style that writes a meter holds every chart in that meter at three levels, and with six styles writing compound meters that had become the push suite's last second. **The push suite could not be timed at the merge** (two builds running: 21.5 and 24.7 s at load 8 to 10, where each style's own tests alone take 5 to 12 s): time it on a quiet machine before the next thing adds takes.
- **Disco's tempo in six and twelve is 100 to 136, its 4/4 range, not two thirds of it**: the builder's reasoning is that the dancer's pulse is the pulse (the kick comes as often as in four). His ear decides; it is one line.
- **Also from the build**: acoustic's upstroke no longer catches a half step on the bottom of the hand (`guitar.ts`; goldens and scorecards unchanged); the tests' soloist kinds and held takes borrow a chart at the borrower's tempo, as the rest already did.
- **Left**: the soloist reads a one-second bar of six as a fast bar and plays a level sparser there (the engine's reading of bar length, not pulses); disco's grip on a written minor ninth sits high.
## the poster wall in motion: a study (stage 6, during gate 6)

Built to `prompts/app-motion-study.md`, with the owner's addition of the same day ("i still want to keep the look uniquely ours"): two more switches whose motion is the poster wall's own (9, 10), and the two most Metro-like things (6, 7) set in our type and inks. A study for his hands, not for merging as it stands. Nothing in the engine, the styles or the audio package changed; no test was added and no journey step changed.

**How it is held together**

- **`packages/app/src/motion/`**: `switches.ts` (the ten switches and the speed, in `localStorage` under `ensemble.motion-study.1`, said to the stylesheet as `m-<id>` classes on `<html>` and `--m-speed`), `moves.ts` (tilt, feather, print, page turn, continuum, the flood), `live.ts` (live tiles), `Switchboard.tsx` (the corner control and `#/motion`). The stylesheet's rules are all under one heading at its end, "The poster wall in motion: a study", each behind its switch's class.
- **All on to begin with; a build made with `VITE_MOTION=off` begins all off.** That is how the smoke journeys are run with the switches off without touching the journeys: `VITE_MOTION=off pnpm smoke` (Playwright's web server inherits the variable and builds with it). Plain `pnpm smoke` runs them with everything on.
- **Every motion is the browser's own animation (`Element.animate`, CSS keyframes on a mark, or a view transition) of `transform` and `opacity` only**, started in the frame of the press or of the screen's first render. Nothing waits on one; a new press finishes or cancels what is under way. Every one checks `prefers-reduced-motion` at the moment it would start; the marks' keyframes are inside a `no-preference` query.
- **The corner control is a 15 by 44 px slip in the top right corner, inside the page's right gutter**, where nothing of the app's is pressed on any screen. A 30 px square there covered the end of "More" on a chart's screen. It opens the switchboard as a sheet; `#/motion` is the same board as a page.
- **The address keeps what the screens are drawn for (`shown`) apart from where the app is (`current`)**, and `onArrival` lets the study tell the screens itself, from inside a view transition. Without the study the two are always the same and the screens are told at once, as before.

**What each was decided to be**

- **1. Tilt**: the innermost of tile, poster, bill, a row's play button, the poster's Play, the dock's Play, a player, a sheet's option or menu line. Up to 10° on a small thing and 4° on a large one (600/size, then 900/size after a first look at 2.7° on a tile), scale 0.97 to 0.985, perspective three times the thing's size; written inline in the `pointerdown`, sprung back over 100 ms with a 0.4 % overshoot. Not the switchboard's own buttons.
- **2. Feather**: home's poster, bills, the first charts' rows and tiles, any list's rows, a sheet's options. Each moves 28 px from the right and fades in over 110 ms; pieces at the same height come together, a line every 22 ms, and from the fifth line on the rest come with the fifth: in by 198 ms. Only what is in view at the first frame. Not in a sheet over a chart while the band plays. A search being typed is not an arrival.
- **3. Continuum**: a same-document view transition with the page itself not captured (`view-transition-name: none` on the root while it runs), so only the pair moves and the rest changes at once; `pointer-events: none` on the transition's layer. The pair is the words of a link to where the press leads (a tile's name, a row's or the poster's title) and the heading there (a style's poster name, a chart's title), or going up, the heading left and the link to it on the screen arrived at. Not from a play button: the band would start a frame later for it. A press during one skips it.
- **4. Page turn**: the screen's root moves in 32 px over 150 ms. Back is the browser's history or a screen higher than the one left (home, the library, then a chart, a take, credits); a move between parts of the library is forward. Edit and the chart are one screen.
- **5. Live tiles**: a tile's back is one of two facts on its own field of ink, "6 charts written for blues" or "Last played in blues" with the chart's title (where one has been played in that style); each turn to the back shows the next fact. The turn is the tile's `rotateX` to edge-on and round, the faces' opacity swapped at the middle. 6 to 10 s apart, a tile not the last, in view, not pressed, nothing turning; none while a dialog is open or a chart's screen is playing, none while the page is hidden.
- **6. Pivot**: at every width, not only a phone, since it is a study and a laptop's strip already fits one row: the words in the poster's capitals (2.75 rem, 3.25 from 900 px), the place shown in full ink with a 4 px rule under it, the rest at 36 %, on the field the strip stood on. Scrolled so the place shown is at the left edge on arrival (again once the type's letters are in). Each style's own bill colour is gone from the strip with it: a style's ink as type on paper is what the palette rules out.
- **7. Oversized titles, on a phone only (under 900 px)**: home's ENSEMBLE at 28 vw (about 1.1 times the screen), with Charts and Takes under its right end on a line of their own; a style's name at 1.3 times the measure it filled. Not on a laptop: a name wider than 1280 px would be taller than the screen. It costs a phone's first rows their place (figures below); the brief's "only where it does not cost the chart list its place" is not met on a phone either, and is left for the owner to weigh rather than the switch made to do nothing.
- **8. The flood**: with 8 or 9 on, the dock stays paper and the style's ink is a field laid under its words (`.flood`), so it can arrive by transform. 8: from Play's own box to the dock's in 120 ms. The dock's words take the press ink at the press, as before, so for those 120 ms the words outside the risen ink are press ink on paper (in the dark theme, dark on dark).
- **9. Print**: the poster, the bills, the tiles in view and a style's head land from 3 % oversize and nothing to size and full in 120 ms, all at once; the flood from 2 %. Where 2 and 9 are both on, the ink fields are printed and the rest feathered; where 8 and 9 are both on, the flood is printed.
- **10. Marks**: the underlines of the sentence's parts (drawn when the chart's screen opens and when the band stops and they come back), the strip's frame or All's or Yours' fill round the place shown (the pivot's rule, with 6), and a sheet's chosen option (the filled block, or a style's frame), each laid as a mark of its own and drawn by `scaleX` from its left edge in 120 ms. Never the chart's marks.

**Measured (headless Chrome 157, the dev server for the motions, the built app for timings)**

- A style's page, first chart row from the top at 390 by 844: jazz 443 (as is), 409 with the pivot, 490 with the titles, 456 with both; neo-soul 366, 332, 391, 357; acoustic 364, 330, 388, 354. The pivot saves 34 px, not "much higher": it is 60 px where the wrapped strip was 94, and the name and the two lines under it are as they were. Getting far higher would take the pivot's word standing for the poster's name, a design call left to the owner. Home's poster with the titles: 142 to 244.
- Continuum: the screen's DOM changes 32 ms after the press (the browser captures the old screen first), where it otherwise changes in the press's task.
- **`out/motion-study/`** (pictures, frames and the probe that took them) is ignored by git.
- Smoke journeys: 5 of 5 with every switch on (`pnpm app:build && pnpm smoke`, 12.0 s) and with every switch off (`VITE_MOTION=off pnpm smoke`, 11.6 s; that build's switches start false, checked in its script). `pnpm check` passes (19.2 s of 20; a run while a live render and two servers shared the machine went to 21.7 s, with nothing changed in what it tests).
- Late notes, `pnpm render:audio … jazz-rhythm-changes-bb.chart live=1`, before and after: 3088 on time, 0 late, 0 dropped, both. It does not open the app's screens.
- The built app, every switch on and every switch off, four runs each: a walk of entrances (home, a style, a chart, back, back, the library, another style) had one long task of 63 to 201 ms in every run either way (the chart's screen being made), and in one run with everything on four (to 195 ms) and a 317 ms frame. Twelve seconds of the band playing with players pressed and More opened and closed: no frame over 20 ms in any run, no long task.

## the poster wall in motion (stage 6, during gate 6)

His verdict on the study, tried on his iPhone 17 with every switch on, 2026-11-16: **"looks great! the live tiles are a bit much, but everything else looks wonderful"**. Then, after more time with it: **"on mobile, the app name is cut off on an iphone 17. we should scale appropriately"**. Built to `prompts/app-motion-build.md` with that note applied: what he saw with everything on at the speed "as specified", less the live tiles, with no title cut by the screen's edge. **The earlier rule "nothing moves on load" (the poster wall's briefs, parts 1 to 3 and the tidy) is relaxed by his word.** What replaces it, now in `docs/BRIEF.md` section 11: motion is short and eased out, says where a thing came from, never delays a press, and is off where the device asks for less. Only `transform` and `opacity` animate.

**How it is held together**

- **`packages/app/src/motion.ts`** (the study's `motion/moves.ts`, less its switches): the tilt, the arrival (turn, print, feather), a sheet's options, the dock's flood, the continuum. `App.tsx` calls `arrived` from a layout effect, `Sheet.tsx` calls `stageSheet`, `Stage.tsx` calls `flood`; `address.ts` keeps the study's hold on an arrival (`shown`, `onArrival`), which the continuum needs. The rules in `style.css` stand where their subjects are (the pivot with the library's places, the marks with the sentence and the sheet, the flood with the dock), with no `html.m-*` gate.
- **The times and distances are tokens at the top of the stylesheet** (`--move-*`); `motion.ts` reads the ones it plays itself once, on first use, so there is one place to change them.
- **Taken out**: the switches, the switchboard and its corner slip, `#/motion`, the speed, `VITE_MOTION`, the live tiles (`live.ts`, the tiles' backs and their styles), the flood's wipe from Play (8; with 9 on it was printed, so he never saw it), the marks' rules for the wrapped strip (they applied only with the pivot off). The stored switches (`ensemble.motion-study.1`) are removed from a browser that has them, at start (`main.tsx`).

**Each motion as it is**

- **Tilt**: as the study's (a tile, the poster and its bills, a row's play button, Play, a player, a sheet's option or menu line; 4 to 10 degrees by size; written in the `pointerdown`'s own task; back in 100 ms with a 0.4 % overshoot). Not on Play in the editing mode's dock. The old `:active` `translateY(1px)` is gone from the buttons that tilt, kept for them under reduced motion and for Play in the editor's dock, where they do not tilt.
- **Arrival**: the screen's root turns in 32 px over 150 ms (from the right going forward, the left going back; not on the first screen); the ink fields in view (poster, bills, tiles, a style's head) are printed from 3 % oversize in 120 ms; a list's rows in view are feathered 28 px from the right over 110 ms, a line every 22 ms, the fifth line and after together. **Not into the editing mode** (an address `#/edit/…` arrived at directly: New chart, Edit a copy): the keyboard may be coming up, and the frame holds still for it.
- **Continuum**: as the study's, 200 ms, only the pair captured. Not from a play button, not into or out of the editing mode, and **a press while a flight is under way ends it**: before the browser has captured the screen being left, the screen arrived at is put in the DOM in the press's own task rather than after the capture. Where the browser has no view transitions the screen changes at once (measured with the API removed: the screen changed in the address's own task).
- **Sheets**: rise as before (140 ms, 28 px); their options feather as on arrival, never while the band plays.
- **Marks**: the sentence's underlines, the pivot's rule under the place shown, a sheet's chosen option, each drawn by `scaleX` from its left in 120 ms. **Not in the editing mode** (the sentence comes and goes there as the keyboard does), and **not in a sheet while the band plays** (`:root:has(.play:not([data-state="idle"]))`): there nothing new moves.
- **The flood**: the dock stays paper and a field of the style's ink (`.flood`) is printed under its words from 2 % oversize in 120 ms at Play's press. It goes at once at Stop.
- **The pivot**: the library's places as one row of large words that scrolls sideways, at every width, as he saw it; on a laptop it keeps to the page's width.
- **Titles**: see below; the study's oversized titles are not kept.

**The mended things**

- **An arriving screen is readable from its first frame.** A feathered piece starts at half its opacity, 28 px out (it was nothing, held invisible up to 88 ms by its place in the stagger); the stagger is unchanged. The printed fields start at half too (they started from nothing, so a style's head was blank in its first frame), and so does the dock's flood (from nothing, the dark theme's dock words were press ink on dark paper for its first frames). Frames in `out/motion-build/c2-*` show the rock page's head and rows legible in the first frame after the press.
- **The continuum's "about 45 px" was not reproduced.** Read frame by frame (Chrome 157, the animations slowed to a tenth and a quarter by the devtools protocol), the first frame of the screen arrived at is offset by exactly the page turn (32 px), and its rows by the turn and their own feather together (60 px, at half opacity); the flight starts where the pressed words were, within 6 px (the tilt under the finger is in what is captured), at scrolls of 0, 45, 153 and 300 px, and ends on the heading, which the browser follows as the turn settles. Run on the study's own commit the frames are the same. The likeliest source of the figure is the turn and feather read together at a slowed frame; nothing was changed for it.
- **The hold**: kept (the screen changes a frame or two after the press when a flight runs, 26 to 70 ms in headless Chrome, noisy); play buttons never fly; Play, Stop and a mute do not change the address, and during a flight the page under it is live and pressable.
- **Titles: a title is never cut by the screen's edge (his note).** Home's ENSEMBLE on a phone is fitted to the width inside the gutters, measured in the real letters (`useLettersIn`, `posterWidth` in `PosterName.tsx`, the same fitting as a style's name); until the letters are in it is at its old size, so it is never fitted to a guess. Charts and Takes stand under its right end, where the study put them: the fitted name leaves no room beside it. **A style's name on its page is the fitted poster name again** (the engineer's reading of his note, applied because it was cut the same way; his to overrule). A laptop is unchanged.
- **Measured at 390 by 844 (dev server, Chrome 157)**: home's poster from the top, main 142, the study 244, now 227. A style's first chart row, main / the study / now: jazz 443 / 456 / 409, neo-soul 366 / 357 / 332, acoustic 364 / 354 / 330. The name's letters end 4 px inside the right gutter at 320, 375, 390, 402 and 430 wide (89 px type at 390); with the web font refused they are at the old size and end at 236 of 374.
- **The name twice on a style's page** (the pivot's word and the poster): left, as he saw it. Removing one would take the poster name off on a phone and the pivot's word standing as the page's heading (its `h1`, and the continuum landing on it), giving the head's ink field over to the two lines under it: the name's 159 px box and its 12 px margin back at 390 (jazz's first row near 240).
- **The editing mode and the sheets**: walked on a phone and a laptop with every animation listed as it started (`out/motion-build/walk.ts`). In the mode, nothing animates entering it, pressing a bar, typing, Enter or Escape, Text or Chart; the two that did, and were switched off there, were the sentence's marks (redrawn each time the keyboard took the line away and gave it back) and the arrival's turn into a `#/edit/` address. Every sheet (played as, key, tempo, how long, More, More in the mode) rises and feathers its options in view when the band is idle; while it plays More only rises, and a choice made in it does not draw.

**Checked**

- `pnpm check` passes, 18.0 s of 20. The five smoke journeys pass against the built app (`pnpm app:build && pnpm smoke`, 11.6 s); no step changed.
- Late notes, `pnpm render:audio … jazz-rhythm-changes-bb.chart live=1`: 3088 on time, 0 late, 0 dropped, on main (`8d0075c`) and on this branch.
- The built app at 390 by 844 with touch, eight runs each of a walk (home, the jazz tile, a row, Play, a mute and back, More and Done, Stop), long tasks (over 50 ms) and long animation frames (over 50 ms): **entrances**, this branch two long tasks in eight runs (82, 86 ms) and one to four long frames a run (51 to 117 ms); main four long tasks (83 to 113 ms) and none to two long frames a run (77 to 118 ms). **While playing and at Stop**, this branch none in any run; main one run with two (137, 70 ms). The arrivals cost frames as the screen changes, none while the band plays.
