## the soloist's pickups (stage 6, during gate 6)

An Opus build from `prompts/solo-pickups.md` (in the builder's clone, `.work/pickups/prompts/`). Brandon, 2026-11-17: "are there any obvious musical devices and techniques the soloist is still missing? ... thinking about pick-up notes in particular".

**SO-37, restated.** A pickup lives only in the breath the phrase before left: its first note comes the style's breath (or a horn's) after the last note before it, judged as heard (swing carries a note's end on an "and" later), and that note is never shortened to make room. Inside a solo it lives in a bar of rest. Into a chorus's first phrase and into the solo's first phrase it may live in the bar before wherever the band plays that bar: the band's own last bar, the soloist's chorus before, the turnaround. Never in a count-in (so with `soloFromTop` the first pass's first phrase starts on its bar, or late where its line does), in a bar no seat but the soloist plays, in an ending, or over the soloist's own last notes without the breath. A pickup is half its bar at most, and where the air will not take the figure drawn, each shorter one is tried, longest first, before none.

**What the engine gained.**

- *A pickup figure of more than a beat* (`Pickup.cell`, was `beat`): a whole number of the bar's last pulses, half the bar at most (in 4/4 two beats, in 12/8 two pulses, in 6/8 one, in 6/4 three beats), and `levels`, the levels it is played at (left out, every level). `validateSoloStyle` holds both. Half a bar, not a bar: every example the brief gives fits in half, and a bar's notes all in its later half is what the critic can read as a pickup (`idea`, `pickupsOf`) without mistaking a line for one: no line rhythm of any style starts in the later half of its bar in 4/4, 12/8 or 6/4 (in 6/8 the later half is the last pulse, as before).
- *A pickup spells the bar before's chord*: walked back from the line's first note a rung at a time, each note a rung on from the one after it; a note on a beat, held, or on a change is a tone of the chord it is played over (the next rung out, or the nearest tone), and a figure that cannot be is not played. Its last note a step or a third from the first, as SO-36 has it.
- *Into the top of a pass* (`passPickup`, `opensPass`). What a pass plays may not hang on the pass after it: "a jam made longer changes nothing already played" and "a jam told to finish plays everything before its last pass as it would have" are the band's own rules, and the app splices on them. A pickup walked to the next pass's first note made both fail (told to finish, the solo's last chorus is another phrase; made longer, the next pass is not in the plan). So a pickup into a pass's first bar is made by the pass before, from what it knows: the bar it is played in, the chord of the bar it leads to, where the soloist last stopped and the register of the phrase before (or, from the band's bar, a fifth round where a solo opens). The phrase it leads to then opens on the one on the note the pickup leads to (`phrasesFor`'s `led`: a shape that opens with a line bar, a first rhythm that opens on the one, and only candidates that keep that note). In an open jam the last pass plays the pickup into the look-ahead's first bar, as the longer jam will. So that a line that would have started late mostly still does, a pickup into a pass is drawn at the style's chance times the share of the level's rhythms that open on the one.
- *A note that leads into a bar its seat plays is played where the seat sits out* (`WrittenNote.leadsIn`, `perform`): the soloist's pickup in the band's bar before a solo. Nothing else sets it.
- *A new solo remembers where the last one stopped* (`restEnds`), so a pickup into it leaves that note its breath.
- *The critic reads a pickup from any bar into a slot* (`pickupsOf`): notes struck in the later half of a bar, after a breath, that run on without one into a note on the next downbeat. `phrasesOf` gives them to the slot they lead into, across a chorus too (before, only the last beat, and only inside a chorus); `idea` drops a bar whose notes are all in its later half (was: in its last beat); `empty-bars` does not count a pickup as playing; `entered-early` counts the solo's first phrase.
- *A pickup is played at the line's level less its own*, whatever the phrase it leads to (not the top note's level): the pickup into a pass cannot know whether the phrase it leads to is the climb.
- *The setting* (`soloDensity`): the last build's design moves the levels and the room, not the habits, so a pickup's chance and length are the style's at every setting. A pickup lives in room, so a busier setting, giving up room, gives up some pickups.

**What each style writes** (4/4; then its other meters). Chances are of a phrase that would start on the one after a breath.

- *Jazz* (0.85, SO-36): the "and" of 4 (2), two eighths (1), as before; three eighths from the "and" of 3 at medium and busy (1), the way ST-jazz-35's lines start, off the beat in eighths; half a bar of eighths at busy (0.5). 6/4 the same, from beats 5 and 6.
- *Blues* (0.65, SO-36): the "and" of 4, two eighths, the triplet on 4 (1 each), as before; three eighths from the "and" of 3 at medium and up (0.5, ST-blues-39's lines from an "and"); two triplets on 3 and 4 at busy and flying (0.5). 12/8: the last eighth, the long-short, the triplet (1 each); from pulse 3, the long-short then the triplet at medium and up (0.5), six eighths at busy and flying (0.5).
- *Rock* (0.45, rising 0.75): the "and" of 4 (1); three eighths up into the one (2, the brief's own); half a bar of eighths at medium and busy (1); the "and" of 3 and a sixteenth burst on 4 at busy (1, ST-rock-22's bursts). 12/8: the last eighth (1), a pulse's three eighths (2), and from medium two pulses (1). ST-rock-24 said none because none was built for rock; the owner asked.
- *Funk* (0.3, approach on): the "a" of 4 (2, the bass's last-sixteenth approach, ST-funk-21/39), the "and" and "a" (1), three sixteenths e-and-a at medium and busy (1, ST-funk-30's busy runs), the "and" of 3 and a sixteenth run on 4 at busy (0.5). 12/8 and 6/8, on the eighths, clipped: the last eighth (2), two (1), a pulse at medium and busy (1); in 12/8 two pulses at busy (0.5). A horn section mostly hits the one: three in ten.
- *Disco* (0.3): the "and" of 4 (2), its "and" and "a" (1), the "and" of 3 through the "a" of 4 at medium and busy (1). 12/8: the last eighth (2), two (1), two pulses at busy (0.5); 6/8 the first two.
- *Neo-soul* (0.2, approach on): seldom, since its line enters late, on the "e"s and "a"s (ST-neosoul-31): the "a" of 4 (2), the "e" and "a" (1), the "a" of 3 and the "e" and "a" of 4 at medium and busy (0.5). 12/8 and 6/8: the last eighth (2), two (1); 12/8 two pulses at busy (0.5).
- *Reggae* (0.15): seldom, since its line starts after the One (ST-reggae-25): the "and" of 4 (2, where the answer bar's cross-stick pushes, ST-reggae-30), two eighths (1), three eighths at medium and busy (0.5). 12/8 and 6/8: the last eighth (2), the long-short (1). Its sparse level has no rhythm that opens on the one, so it never leads into the top of a pass.
- *Acoustic* (0.35): a sung line's anacrusis: the "and" of 4 (2), two eighths (1), two quarters on 3 and 4 at medium and busy (1), in its quarters and eighths (ST-acoustic-26). 6/8: the last eighth (2, the six-eight tune's own), the long-short (1), the pulse's three eighths at medium and busy (1). The old 6/8 figure that could not play is replaced.

**Measured** (`out/solo-pickups/src/measure.ts`: each style's own charts and its standards as a jam of four passes with the soloist, six seeds; a phrase is what the soloist plays after a breath; before / on / after the barline, starting commit → branch). Jazz 14.1/40.0/46.0 → 17.8/40.5/41.7; blues 11.4/63.5/25.1 → 18.8/61.5/19.7; rock 0/83.9/16.1 → 13.3/72.8/13.8; funk 0/65.6/34.4 → 7.8/58.0/34.2; disco 0/82.0/18.0 → 9.2/72.7/18.1; neo-soul 0.1/33.3/66.6 → 4.2/30.5/65.3; reggae 0/9.6/90.4 → 0.2/9.4/90.4; acoustic 0/77.1/22.9 → 13.3/69.2/17.6. Chorus tops led into, of those where the band plays the bar before: 0 everywhere → jazz 95/324, blues 42/108, rock 25/126, funk 26/144, acoustic 15/126, disco 13/108, neo-soul 12/126, reggae 0/126. What holds the share down in the blues and jazz is SO-37's air: an end bar's note is cut to leave the breath before the barline, so after an end bar there is seldom a breath's air left for a pickup, and most live after a bar of rest.

**Properties.** New: "the soloist leads phrases in" (the share of phrases after a breath that start before their barline, a floor by style: jazz 0.18, blues 0.13, rock 0.1, funk and disco 0.05, neo-soul 0.015, acoustic 0.08, reggae at least one); "the top of a chorus, and of the solo, is led into some of the time ... never from a count-in, and never by more than half a bar"; "a pickup spells the chord it is played over" (into a phrase of the form, where a line started late cannot be mistaken for one). Restated: "the soloist plays only where it has the lead" (a pickup from the later half of the bar before, in a bar of music); "a song is played as it is arranged" (the same, for the soloist in the section before the solo); "a three-chorus solo builds" (a pickup counted with the chorus it leads into); the setting's order allows notes a second to fall by a hundredth of themselves and the share sounding by a hundredth of the lead from one setting to the next (a busier setting gives up room, and the pickups in it: on the full run the blues' share sounding went 0.648 → 0.644 from a quarter to a half, jazz's notes a second 3.02 → 3.02 from three quarters to 1; with no pickups the blues' is in order).

**Goldens.** The six with a soloist whose solo moved are rewritten (blues, funk, jazz, rock, disco, acoustic): pickups, and the phrases that follow them. Neo-soul's and reggae's with a soloist, and all nine without one, are byte for byte. In all eight the drums' and the bass's events are as they were; the chords differ only in the blues (188 events), jazz (180) and rock (2), all inside the soloist's lead bars.

**The comp in a bar with a pickup into a lead.** The bar stays the band's (it is no lead bar, and nothing in the plan changes), but a comp that listens (the blues, jazz, rock, neo-soul) hears the pickup: over each style's own charts, three seeds, of 31 band bars with a pickup in them the comp plays differently in 21 (the same rhythm with other voicings in most, kept off the soloist's notes; another rhythm in a few, and in four bars the bar before the pickup's too, the comp choosing its phrase by what it hears). Funk, disco and acoustic do not listen: their comp is unchanged. The comp's seat was not in the brief; left.

**Scorecards** rewritten in every style. Asked for: `solo.entered-early` up everywhere (jazz 0.16 → 0.25, blues 0.14 → 0.28, rock 0 → 0.24, funk 0 → 0.19, acoustic 0 → 0.17, disco 0 → 0.13, neo-soul 0 → 0.06, reggae 0 → 0.003); the targets of the six that had `max: 0` (no pickups, a tripwire) are now ceilings, SO-39's "never most": rock 0.5, funk and acoustic 0.45, disco 0.4, neo-soul 0.3, reggae 0.15. Jazz's `max 0.5` in target 0.96 → 0.91. Following from leading into the solo's first phrase and opening it on the one: `solo.builds` lower (rock in target 0.75 → 0.65, disco 0.80 → 0.73, past the tolerance; jazz's mean 1.74 → 1.68): the first chorus is busier by its pickups and its opening. Every metric that moved is in the build's report.

**The whole suite at the setting's ends and from the top** (six seeds, every take with the soloist given the setting or `soloFromTop` by a variable read in `test/takes.ts` for the run and then removed). Everywhere "the same ... seed give the identical performance" fails, because the variable reaches the suite's takes and not the property's second render: the probe's, not the band's. At 0: "the soloist is not sparse" in seven styles (its floors are the default's). At 1: "the soloist leads phrases in" in the blues (9.3% under 13%), rock (8.4% under 10%) and jazz (14.7% under 18%), since a busier setting gives up the room pickups live in; and "a comp that listens makes room" in the blues, rock and jazz, as at the last build. From the top: "a comp that listens makes room" in rock, as at the last build. Nothing else.

## the soloist's pickups: checked and merged (stage 6, 2026-11-17)

- **Read**: the diff (the performer's `leadsIn`, the pickup into a pass made from what the pass before knows, each style's figures), the builder's tables, the goldens (drums and bass the same in all eight with a soloist; nine without one byte for byte). Run on main after the merge, quiet machine: `pnpm check` 23.1 s of 24; `pnpm test:full` 61.5 s of 72; `pnpm score` 62.1 s of 120, no movement.
- **Half a bar at most, not a bar**: the builder's call, so that the critic can tell a pickup from a line. Kept.
- **A pickup into the top of a pass forces that phrase to open on the one**, so that an open jam made longer and a jam told to finish play what they played. The cost is fewer late starts at chorus tops (jazz 46.0% of phrases start after the barline to 41.7%; the blues 25.1 to 19.7). Kept: the app splices takes on that guarantee. For his ear.
- **Reggae is led in almost never** (0.2% of phrases; its lines start late nine times in ten, and its sparse level has nothing that opens on the one). As written; for his ear.
- **Jazz and the blues are under the notes' own targets** (17.8% against 20 to 30; 18.8% against 25 to 50): a phrase's last note is already cut for its breath, so a pickup mostly follows a bar of rest. Left.
- **The setting's order now allows a fall of a hundredth** between settings: a busier setting gives up the room pickups live in. At 1 the blues, rock and jazz lead in less than their floors (9.3%, 8.4%, 14.7%); the suite does not play that end.
- **The solo builds less again** in rock (in target 75% to 65%) and disco (80 to 73): a first chorus led into opens on the one. With the last build's fall, this is now the soloist's largest known fault; the cure is still a busier top.
- **A comp that listens hears a pickup in the band's bar** and moves its voicing off it (21 of 31 such bars). Left; the comp was not in the brief.
- **The push suite is at 23.1 s of 24 again.** Before the four style-meters: find time in the suite, since each adds charts to every style's run. The six-seed run once lost a style's process to memory on the builder's side (eight processes of 1.2 GB on 6.9 GB); it did not on main. Watch it.

## where the push suite's time goes (stage 6, 2026-11-17)

- **Measured, with pickups merged**: typecheck 0.1 s, lint 0.4 s, the suite 23 s. One style alone is 11.4 s (funk), four together 16 s, all nine processes 23.5 s on eight virtual cores. Running fewer at once is slower (26.8 to 28.5 s at four to seven); a larger young generation, single-threaded collection and lazy compilation change nothing. So the suite's time is the machine's whole work, not its slowest style's, and not memory.
- **In one style's run** (a profile of funk): rendering takes is 7.3 s of 11.4, the soloist the dearest seat (2.0 s), then the bass (1.5); the second render that checks a take is the same take again is 2.7 s of that.
- **Not acted on.** The four style-meters fit: jazz's third meter takes the budget to 26 s by the rule. If time is wanted later, the first place to look is the second render (every take twice), then the soloist's critic.

## the blues in six (stage 6, during gate 6)

An Opus build from `prompts/meter-compound-style.md` in its own clone on the branch `bluesrock68`. The blues' four seats carry `meters["6/8"]` beside the 12/8 the owner passed; `WRITTEN` in `test/takes.ts` has `blues: ["12/8", "6/8"]`; one chart in six. Nobody has heard it: the page for his ear is `out/meter-study-5-blues/` (not committed; `src/` has the script and the grid printer).

**Which it is: the blues counted in two, as a gospel blues ballad is.** The blues' compound tradition is the slow blues in twelve, already written. Its tradition in six is the gospel and soul blues ballad, felt in two slow beats a bar: the drummer's triplets on the cymbal, the kick on the One, the backbeat on the second beat, the piano's triplets. That is what is written, taken from the slow blues in twelve (the same players, the same cymbal, box, chops and soloist's long-short and triplet) and from the rule for a bar of two (SU-D-14: there is no beat 3; the second pulse is the backbeat's). Nothing swings and nothing is struck between the eighths.

**What each seat plays** (a bar is twelve sixteenths in two beats of three eighths; only the eighths are struck):

- *Drums.* Derived from twelve (`halfOf`, its first half): the cymbal's triplets (quiet `o...g. o...g.`, from the working level `x.o.o. x.o.o.`, hot the hat or the ride with the hat foot on the backbeat, a section's choice for the pass), the backbeat on the second beat (a cross-stick under 0.30), the lope (the backbeat's last eighth lighter). Written for six: the kick on the One, pushed softly on the bar's last eighth into the next (quiet 1 section in 4, working 3 in 5, hot always), hot a feathered kick under the backbeat (3 in 5); the snare's ghost on the last eighth (working 3 in 10, hot 2 in 5) or the triplet ghosted round the backbeat (hot 1 in 5); the hat breathing open on the last eighth of an answer bar (35% working, 60% hot). Fills an eighth or a beat, never longer: an eighth (the snare, the floor, both hands), a beat that starts on the backbeat (the triplet on it, "four, AND", crack-boom, the triplet onto the floor, round the kit in one beat, both hands flat), so no bar of six loses its backbeat; phrase none quiet, an eighth working, a beat hot; section an eighth quiet, a beat above. The kick and the snare together are on three of the six eighths at most (the One, the backbeat, the push; hot the feather is on the backbeat).
- *Bass.* The box at twelve's speed, a tone a beat, so a bar of six is half a bar of the box: each shape's two bars of four tones are four bars of two (`inTwos`), up through the first two bars on a chord and down through the next two (derived: the same line, cut at the half bar). Working, a section's figure: both beats plain (2), the lope into the One (3), the lope into the backbeat (2); hot, both beats lope (`x---o- x---o-`). Quiet in two as twelve is, a half bar of twelve being a bar of six: the bass note held the bar and its fifth the next bar on the chord. Lead-ins are the box's, on the last beat. The bar-long walks are four notes and a bar of six has two, so in six there are none.
- *Piano.* Written for a bar of two. Quiet, twelve's pad (held; held and a lean on the last eighth; the One and the first beat's last eighth; answered by the two beats). Working: the chop on the backbeat (3), the triplet on the backbeat (2), the One and the push into the backbeat (2); answered by a late one on the last eighth or the push and a chop. Hot: the triplets all bar in close position (2, a groove, as twelve's and the pump are) or the triplet on the backbeat (2), answered by the One and the backbeat's triplet or the push into it: every hot figure has the backbeat's triplet. Under a soloist it stays in its working book, as in four and twelve.
- *Soloist.* Written for a bar of two from twelve's material (the beat held, the long-short, the triplet): four levels by four's names and notes a bar (the critic asks a bar of six three quarters of them), cells of 1.5, 2.8, 4.5 and 5 notes; twelve's shapes; endings land in the first beat and leave the second unstruck; pickups twelve's last-beat three (the last eighth, the long-short, the triplet), half a bar being a beat; the top note held a beat and most of the next.

**Properties.** Restated so they mean something in six, and held in four, six and twelve: *the time* (the kick on the bar's strong pulses, a backbeat on every pulse between; a long fill may take the last, and in six, where no fill is longer than a pulse, every bar keeps its own); *the offbeat eighth is the last of a triplet* (in six and twelve played where written); *nothing between the triplet's notes*; *the cymbal plays the triplets* (from the working level every eighth of every bar of time, quiet the pulses and never the middle eighth; held in six as in twelve). Added: *the bass is a tone a pulse and lopes into the next* (in six and twelve: quiet only on the pulses, from the working level every pulse of a bar of one chord, hot a note on every pulse's last eighth, never the triplet's middle eighth); *in six the piano plays the gospel blues* (away from a soloist a quiet bar of one chord struck twice at most beside a lean into the next chord; hot every bar of one chord strikes its backbeat and one of its later eighths: before a change the engine gives the old chord's middle eighth, or its last, to the new one). Adding the blues' 6/8 to `WRITTEN` holds it to the every-style rules for a style's own parts too. **Against the fit** (the chart in the library, no data for six): all five failed: the time (a 4/4 fill took the backbeat in a bar of six), nothing between the triplet's notes (a 4/4 fill's snare on a sixteenth), the cymbal (no middle eighth at the working level), the bass (no lope hot), the piano (no backbeat struck hot). The new bass property passed in twelve as it was.

**Decided here, because the brief and the notes did not settle it:**

- *Tempo in six: the default, two thirds of four's, 40 to 100, usually 67; nothing written in `tempo.meters`.* Twelve was given its own range because a shuffle already is a bar of twelve; a bar of six at the shuffle's 150 would be 0.8 s, so four's range does not carry over. The chart is at 60, the slow blues' figure in twelve.
- *The kick and the snare are held to half the eighths by the writing*: the push into the One is the kick's only off-beat stroke, the feather sits under the backbeat, the snare's other strokes are ghosts.
- *A fill in six is a beat at most and starts on the backbeat* (DR-46: a fill of a beat or less keeps the backbeat). Twelve's two-beat fills would be the whole bar but its One.
- *The box is cut, not rewritten*: the line in six moves at twelve's pace, a tone a beat, so a chord of one bar plays the box's first two tones and its sixth comes only on a stay of two bars (`bass.sixths-on-dominants` reads 0% on the chart, below).
- *The quiet bass is a note a bar*, not a note a beat: twelve's two-feel is a note a half bar, and the property and the drums' "quiet is thinner" ask quiet to be thinner than working.
- *The chart* (`library/blues-gospel-six-eb.chart`, "Gospel Blues in Six"): E-flat, 6/8 at 60, sixteen bars, `Eb7 | Ab7 | Eb7 | % | Ab7 | Abm6 | Eb7 | C7 | Fm7 | Bb7 | Eb7 | C7 | Fm7 | Bb7 | Eb7 Ab7 | Eb7 Bb7`: the quick change, the four going minor (the gospel minor four), the six into the two, the two and five said twice, the second time into a turnaround of a chord a beat. The blues library was in A, C, D, F, G and A minor. No named tune.

**No engine change.** Everything is data under `meters`.

**A floor moved, not a rule**: the chart moved which charts each style is handed on a push, and acoustic's strums in six (`COMPOUND_STRUMS` in `test/piano.test.ts`) came to 1,317 against the 1,333 the floor asked; the floor is 3,600 for the full run now (1,200 on a push).

**Nothing that exists changed.** The goldens are byte for byte (`pnpm goldens` rewrites none). The eight scorecards, rewritten to a scratch file on their own charts (the blues on its six charts named one by one) with the parts in and the chart in the library, are byte for byte their baselines, as they were at the start. The blues' is accepted with the new chart (`score --style blues --update`).

**The new chart alone, as the blues** (200 takes): the kick on the One, the changes stated, the soloist's changes on chord tones, each 100%; the bass's register 35.4 (34 to 43); the piano's lowest note 60.8, no voicing faults; the soloist's beats on chord tones 93%, a range of 16.7 semitones, 1.39 notes a second in its first chorus and 2.13 in its last, `solo.cost` 1.59 (94% of takes under 1.80). Outside their bands: `bass.sixths-on-dominants` 0% against 15% to 35% (above: a chord of one bar gets the box's first two tones); `bass.half-step-approaches` 17% (45% of takes inside 15% to 40%); `bass.changes-by-step` 31% (55% of takes over 20%); `chords.colour` 79%, 71% of takes inside (the hot triplets are in close position, as twelve's are); `solo.empty-bars` 5%, 52% inside; `solo.builds` 1.55, 65% inside; `solo.high-points-deserved` 73%, 77% inside.

**Every style on the chart as its own** (a copy of the repo with only this chart in its library, its tempo held inside each style's range, every kind of take on six seeds and the soloist's kinds): no rule about what is played failed. What failed is what a one-chart library cannot meet: the floors on how many cases a property saw; the blues' "does not play one line all night", which names `blues-12-bar-a`; "the biggest fill is kept for where the band steps up" in disco (its guard that a step up was met at a chorus's end); and the soloist's own measures over a style's charts: "the soloist is not sparse" (disco 2.06 notes a second under 2.23, funk 1.36 under 1.69, jazz 1.88 under 2.45; a bar of six at 60 to 100 is not a style's own tempo) and "the setting moves the soloist all the way along" (acoustic's ends too close; funk, neo-soul, reggae and rock a hundredth or more down at one quarter; reggae's share sounding under its floor). Each is a mean over a style's own charts and was met here on one chart and three seeds; none was looked into further.

**Timings.** The blues' own tests alone (`ENSEMBLE_STYLES=blues node --test --test-isolation=none test/*.test.ts`, load 1 to 2): 10.0 and 9.9 s at the start commit, 10.5 and 10.4 s on the branch: half a second more, under the 1.5 allowed. `pnpm test`: 23.6 s of 24 on the branch at load 3; another builder then took the load to 46, and the start commit and the branch could not be timed beside each other.

## rock in six (stage 6, during gate 6)

The second build of the same brief in the same clone (`bluesrock68`), after the blues. Rock's four seats carry `meters["6/8"]` beside the 12/8 the owner passed; `WRITTEN` in `test/takes.ts` has `rock: ["12/8", "6/8"]`; one chart in six. Nobody has heard it: the page for his ear is `out/meter-study-5-rock/` (not committed; `src/` has the script and the grid printer, shared with the blues' page).

**Which it is: the rock ballad in six-eight, the heavy two.** Rock has a tradition in six: the six-eight ballad and the slow rock song counted in two (the hat or ride on the six eighths, the kick on the One with a pickup, the snare on the second beat, a bass of roots under the kick, a piano's triplets). That is written, taken from rock in twelve (the same players, cymbal, triplets and lead guitar) and said for a bar of one strong pulse and one answering it (SU-D-14), held to what the notes heard as too busy in six on rock (EAR-11, AU-96). Nothing swings and nothing is struck between the eighths.

**What each seat plays** (a bar is twelve sixteenths in two pulses of three eighths):

- *Drums.* Derived from twelve (`halfOf`): the cymbal (quiet the pulse and a ghost on its last eighth `o...g. o...g.`; working `x.o.o. x.o.o.`; hot, a section's choice, the ride with the hat foot on the backbeat or the hat unaccented on all six) and the backbeat on the second pulse (a cross-stick under 0.30). Written for six: three kicks, each the One and at most one pickup: none (6, 3, 1 by tier), the first pulse's last eighth into the backbeat (2, 4, 4), the bar's last eighth into the next One (1, 3, 4); quiet the pickup soft. The answer bars (2, 4, 6) turn once a section, and the turn moves the kick's pickup rather than add one: quiet a soft kick moved to the last eighth (2) or to the first pulse's last (1); louder the same moves (3, 2), the hat opened on the last eighth (2, on the hat only), or the snare answering its backbeat on the last eighth with the kick's pickups given up (1); where the section's kick already has the turn's eighth, the next audible turn plays. So the kick and the snare are on three of the six eighths at most. Fills: an eighth, the last two eighths, or the last pulse, which is the backbeat's and starts on the snare there; a phrase an eighth quiet and two eighths above, a section two eighths quiet and a pulse above (ST-rock-08's steps, the eight of four capped at a pulse).
- *Bass.* Twelve's line said for a bar of two: quiet the downbeat and wherever the kick plays; working a root on both pulses, a section's pickup into the One (2), into the backbeat (1) or none (2), and every kick's pickup (`followsKick`, as in twelve); hot a pickup into both pulses, the section's octave pops into the backbeat, into the One, both, or none.
- *Piano.* Twelve's book cut (derived): quiet a held chord or a chord a pulse; working and hot the triplets on every eighth, 88 and 70, 92 and 76; under a soloist a chord a pulse (`soloing`).
- *Lead guitar.* Twelve's cells said for a bar of two (a pulse held, the pulse and its last eighth, the triplet), four's levels and notes a bar (the critic asks three quarters of them): cells of 1.5, 3 and 4.5 notes; endings land in the first pulse and leave the second unstruck; pickups twelve's from its last pulse (the last eighth, three eighths up into the One); the top note held a pulse and most of the next.

**Properties.** Restated so they mean something in six, and held in four, six and twelve, rock in six held at three levels on its own chart: *the time* (a backbeat on every pulse between the strong ones, the last of which a fill may take: in six the second pulse); *the cymbal and a quiet bar* (in six and twelve every eighth from the working level; quiet the pulses and at most their last eighths, the kick on the strong pulses and pulses' last eighths, the snare on the backbeats alone); *the bass is the kick's, roots on the pulses* (no more notes than two of every three eighths, every pulse from the working level, hot a pickup into every pulse); *the piano's triplets*; *the whole band on the eighths*. **Against the fit** (the chart in the library, no data): the cymbal (no hat on the second eighth), the piano (no chord on the second eighth), the bass (a kick on the second pulse's middle eighth with no bass under it) and the eighths (a 4/4 fill's snare on a sixteenth) failed; the time passed.

**Decided here, because the brief and the notes did not settle it:**

- *Tempo in six: twelve's, 48 to 107, usually 66* (`tempo.meters["6/8"]`), not the default two thirds of four (53 to 107, 80). The same reasoning as twelve's (his ear passed it there): the ballad's pulse lives lower than 80, and a dotted pulse can go to about 48; the ceiling keeps the eighths no quicker than four's. One line to change.
- *No fifth kick and no kick added by a turn* (EAR-11; the every-style half-the-eighths ceiling).
- *A pulse-long fill starts on the backbeat*: in six it is the bar's only one.
- *The lead guitar's busy level keeps notes held a pulse*: written with every eighth struck it built a little more (`solo.builds` 1.31 over 11 takes) and shook half as often (`solo.shakes` 0.16): taken out again.
- *The chart* (`library/rock-ballad-six-d.chart`, "Rock Ballad in Six"): D, 6/8 at 66, forty-eight bars, verse, chorus, verse, chorus, bridge, chorus. Verse `D | A/C# | Bm | G | D | A/C# | G | A` (the bass walking down from the one through the five over its third); chorus `G | A | D | Bm | G | A | D | G A` (from the four, turning on the four and five a pulse each); bridge `Bm | F#m | G | D | Em | F#m | G | A`. Rock's library was in C, G, A minor, D (a one-bar riff), E-flat (twelve), A and E. No named tune.

**No engine change.**

**Nothing that exists changed.** The goldens are byte for byte. The eight scorecards, rewritten to a scratch file with rock's parts and both new charts in (the blues and rock on their earlier charts named one by one), are byte for byte their baselines at the start. Rock's is accepted with the new chart (`score --style rock --update`).

**The new chart alone, as rock** (200 takes): the kick and the bass on the One, the changes stated, every kick under a bass note, each 100%; the bass's register 35.2, half-step approaches 16%; the piano's lowest note 60.4, no colour, no voicing faults; the soloist's changes on chord tones 100%, beats 88%, phrase ends 96%, a range of 18.2 semitones, 1.49 notes a second in its first chorus and 1.86 in its last. Outside their bands: `solo.builds` 1.25 against 1.50 to 4.00 (3% of takes inside; twelve's chart read 1.50, and the build is the soloist's largest known fault since the pickups); `solo.shakes` 0.27 a bar (36% inside 0.30 to 0.90); `solo.empty-bars` 8% (59% inside); `solo.cost` 2.19 (82% under 2.45).

**Every style on the chart as its own** (as for the blues' chart): no rule about what is played failed in any style but funk, whose "in six and twelve the bass plays the section's riff, not the kick's roots" fails on a jam of two passes, seed 1, bar 88 (A before G, a section fill, at 0.84): the riff's pop on the last eighth is replaced by a lead-in a whole step over the next root, which is A, the bar's own root. The same fault reggae's builder found on reggae's chart in six (funk's data or funk's property); not fixed. Otherwise what failed is what a one-chart library cannot meet: the floors; the blues' rule that names `blues-12-bar-a`; "the biggest fill is kept for where the band steps up" (a one-section-form rule, and the chart has three sections: no case); and the soloist's means over a style's charts ("not sparse": disco 2.20 under 2.23, funk 1.54 under 1.69, jazz 2.05 under 2.45, neo-soul's and reggae's share sounding; "the setting moves the soloist all the way along", a hundredth or more down at one step in acoustic, the blues, funk, jazz and rock).

**Every style on every chart.** `pnpm test:full`'s material, one style to a process at six seeds (the machine was too loaded to run it whole in its budget): every style passes, 468 to 473 tests each, no failure.

**Timings** (load 4 to 9; another builder's work took it to 46 between runs). Rock's own tests alone: 11.2 and 11.1 s at the start commit (`253db7c`), 12.1 and 12.1 s with both styles in: 0.9 s more, under the 1.5 allowed. The blues' alone in the same sitting: 10.5 s at the start, 11.1 s here. **`pnpm test` is over its budget**: 23.6 s at the start commit and 28.3 and 28.1 s on the branch, beside it, of 24 (26 once jazz's third meter is merged). The suite's time is the machine's whole work: each of the nine processes plays the two new charts (borrowed, and in "the drums thin out" every style renders every compound chart held quiet and loud), so each grows by about half a second, not only the blues' and rock's. Not consolidated here; the merge needs to take it back (the first places measured are the second render of every take and the soloist's critic, "where the push suite's time goes").

## the blues and rock in six: merged; the suite's time and memory (stage 6, 2026-11-18)

- **Read**: the builder's report and grids; no engine change; goldens byte for byte; the eight scorecards unmoved on the charts that existed. On main after the merge: the push suite passes in every style (run while another builder had the machine at a load of 10 to 26, so not timed).
- **The six-seed run lost funk's and disco's processes** on that loaded machine: each passes alone (78 and 75, no failure), and swap was full. Nine processes of over a gigabyte each are more than 6.4 GB holds when anything else is running. **`scripts/test.ts` now runs again, alone, a process the machine ended** (a signal, not an exit code): that is no test failing. A failing test is still a failure.
- **The push suite's time is owed a quiet measurement.** The builder read 28.1 to 28.3 s beside 23.6 for the starting commit, under load; the budget is 24 s, and 26 when jazz's third meter lands. To be timed when the jazz build is in, and consolidated then if it is over: nothing is being raised.
- **Known and left**: funk's riff rule fails on rock's chart in six (bar 88: the lead-in a whole step above G is A, the bar's own root, so the bar reads as roots alone), the fault reggae's build found on reggae's chart; `COMPOUND_STRUMS` (acoustic's floor of strums counted in six on a push) lowered from 4,000 to 3,600, a count and not a rule; rock's solo in six builds little (`solo.builds` 1.25, 3% of takes in band).
- **For his ear**: `out/meter-study-5-blues/`, `out/meter-study-5-rock/`. The blues in six is the gospel blues in two; rock in six is the ballad as a heavy two, at twelve's tempo range (48 to 107, usually 66).
## jazz in six and twelve (stage 6, during gate 6)

An Opus build from `prompts/meter-compound-style.md` in its own clone on the branch `jazzcompound`. Jazz's four seats carry `meters["12/8"]` and `meters["6/8"]` beside its `"6/4"`; `WRITTEN` in `test/takes.ts` has `jazz: ["6/4", "6/8", "12/8"]`; one chart in each meter. Nobody has heard it: the page for his ear is `out/meter-study-5-jazz/` (not committed; `src/` has the script and the grid printer).

**Which is which.** Jazz has two compound traditions: the slow twelve-eight ballad, and the Afro-Cuban six-eight that jazz drummers call "six-eight". *Twelve is the ballad*: a bar of four slow pulses, which is a bar of four with each beat a triplet, so it is jazz's own 4/4 said in pulses (AU-97: a beat is a pulse, an "and" the pulse's last eighth), at four's ballad speed (ST-jazz-01: ballads near 66). *Six is jazz's six-eight*: the ride the old band was taught for All Blues in 6/8 (AU-92, EAR-19: each pulse and the eighth before the next, `x...x. x...x.`), which is also the short bell of the Afro-Cuban six; two pulses, the strong one and its answer, written for the bar and not cut from twelve. Nothing swings in either and nothing is struck between the eighths.

**What each seat plays** (counted over six seeds of each chart held at a level, three passes, bars of time without fills):

- *Drums, twelve.* Quiet: four's light head on the closed hat said in pulses (`x..... X...x. x..... X...x.`: each pulse, the skip on the last eighth of 2 and 4), no kick, no comping, only whispers at phrase ends; under 0.3 a ballad's soft ride on the same places. From the working level the ballad's triplets on the ride, every eighth (`x.o.x. X.o.x. ...`, the middle soft), the foot on 2 and 4, the kick feathered on every pulse as on every beat in four, the left hand comping on four's lists with each "and" a pulse's last eighth. Set-ups on the bar's last eighth (the shot and the bomb, which leaves the feathered 4). Fills: four's shapes in eighths, an eighth, a pulse, two pulses (four's 2, 4, 8 sixteenths). 6.2 strokes a bar quiet, 19.1 working, 19.2 hot (the fit 6.2, 13.1, 13.2); the kick and snare on 4.8 to 5.0 of twelve eighths.
- *Drums, six.* AU-92's ride, the second pulse leaned on; the foot on the second pulse (the backbeat of a bar of two, SU-D-14); the kick feathered on the One alone; the left hand on the pulses' middle and last eighths, the push into the second pulse most of all. Quiet the same on the closed hat. Fills an eighth, two eighths, or the second pulse at most. 4.2, 7.1 and 7.3 strokes a bar (the fit 3.2, 6.4, 6.5).
- *Bass.* Quiet in two in both: twelve the bass note on 1 and its fifth on 3, pulse 4 an approach where the lead-in's coin says so (as beat 4 in four); six one on each pulse. Twelve from the working level walks a note a pulse, a section choosing to lean into 3 and the One from the pulse before's last eighth (the ballad bassist's "ba-DUM") by weights 3 none, 2 into the One, 1 both; hot, into 3 and the One (3), into 4 and the One (2), into every pulse (1). Six lilts: the second pulse leaning into the One (3), the One leaning into the second pulse (2), into both (1); hot into both (3). Pitches are walked as in four. 2.6, 4.5, 6.5 notes a bar in twelve; 2.0, 3.0, 3.8 in six.
- *Piano.* Twelve: four's book said in pulses (the Charleston is 1 and the last eighth of 2, pushed into 3), with one answer of the ballad's own at the working level (the triplet leaning into the One) and one hot (into 3). Six: the One and its answer and the eighth before each: the Charleston of a bar of two (the One and the push into the second pulse), the two pulses, both pushed, held chords quiet. 2.0 to 2.4 strikes a bar in twelve, 1.2 to 2.2 in six (the fit: a pad, 2.0 and 1.1).
- *Alto.* Twelve: four's cells said in pulses at sparse; from medium the triplet on one or two pulses; busy mostly triplets; pickups from pulse 4, and from pulse 3 at medium and busy. Six: cells for a bar of two in the long-short and the triplet; pickups from the second pulse. Endings land on a long note and leave the last pulse unstruck. The levels' phrase shapes are one table for every meter. 1.71 notes a second over a jam of four passes on the ballad (4/4 jazz at 60: 1.12 to 1.20), 2.32 on the six.

**Decided here:**

- *Tempo.* Twelve is 40 to 96, usually 66 (`tempo.meters["12/8"]`): two thirds of four's range would be 40 to 160, usually 92, a medium six and not a ballad; 66 is four's ballad with a pulse a beat, and at 60 the soloist sat under its floor of notes a second over jazz's own charts (2.44 of 2.45 on the six-seed run). Six keeps the default, 40 to 160, usually 92: its eighths no quicker than four's. The charts are at 66 and 88.
- *The six's kick is on the One alone* and its foot on the second pulse: two feathers and a comping stroke would be half the bar's six eighths.
- *Twelve's ride from the working level is every eighth*, not the pulse and its skip: the triplet ride is what makes a twelve-eight ballad one. Quiet keeps the skip alone, so quiet is thinner (AU-96).
- *No eighth in six's endings stands alone on the first pulse's last eighth*, and each opens on the One: at 160 a pulse is 0.38 s, under the breath (found by the suite), and a pickup into a phrase that opens on its ending led, on neo-soul's chart in six at 160, into a note an eighth after the barline and was left hanging (found by the six-seed run).
- *The charts.* `library/jazz-ballad-twelve-ab.chart`, "Ballad in Twelve": A-flat, 12/8 at 66, sixteen bars, two chords to most bars: `Abmaj7 Fm7 | Bbm7 Eb7 | Cm7 F7b9 | Bbm7 Eb7 | Abmaj7 Ab7 | Dbmaj7 Gb9 | Cm7 F7b9 | Bbm7 Eb7` and `Abmaj7 Fm7 | Bbm7 Eb7 | Ebm7 Ab7 | Dbmaj7 Dbm6 | Cm7 F7b9 | Bbm7 Eb7 | Abmaj7 F7#9 | Bbm7 Eb7` (the four by its own two-five, the backdoor flat seven, the minor four). `library/jazz-six-eight-em.chart`, "Jazz in Six": E minor, 6/8 at 88, AABA of thirty-two, most chords two bars: A `Em9 | % | Am9 | % | Em9 | % | F#m7b5 | B7b9`; A2 and A3 `Em9 | % | Am9 | % | Cmaj7 | F#m7b5 B7b9 | Em9 | %`; the bridge `Am9 | D13 | Gmaj7 | Cmaj7 | F#m7b5 | B7b9 | Em9 | B7#9`. Jazz's library had no chart in A-flat or E minor. No named tune.

**One engine change, found by acoustic on the chart in six: the picking thumb's alternate bass is never above a string a finger plucks** (`alternate` in `guitar.ts`). Where the chord's fifth lies over the grip's bass it is the alternate bass only on a string no higher than the third from the top, the lowest a finger reaches; otherwise the thumb plays the bass again. Acoustic on F#m7b5 (F#3 A3 C4 E4) with no bassist put the thumb on C4 with the index on A3 under it, against "a guitar picks a bar of six as six-eight is picked" (no finger under the thumb in the pulse). Every golden and every scorecard is byte for byte; acoustic's takes on three borrowed charts in four change (Falling Fifths, Have You Met Miss Jones, Night and Day, the kinds with no bassist: the same crossing there, the thumb's note over the fingers').

**Properties.** Restated in pulses and held in four, 6/4, six and twelve (`jazzPlayed`: jazz's own takes, and its own charts in six and twelve held at each level): *the ride on every pulse once the band opens up, the hat foot on the pulse after each strong one* (2 and 4; 6/4's 2 and 5; six-eight's second pulse), *the snare off the One and off the foot*; *the head on the closed hat*, read on its second pulse; *the band on the eighths* (in a bar of quarter beats nothing between a beat and its "and"; in a bar of dotted pulses nothing between the eighths and nothing swung, the fills and the soloist too). Adding jazz to `WRITTEN` holds it in six and twelve to the every-style rules for a style's own parts, and its soloist's chromatic rule now holds there too. Added: *the cymbal's skip is the pulse's last eighth* (six: the ride on each pulse and its last eighth only; twelve: every eighth; quiet never the middle eighth); *the bass plays the pulses and the triplet's last eighth as a pickup* (never the middle eighth, the strong pulses always, quiet in two, from the working level a note on every pulse and never one pitch twice running, hot a pickup in every bar of one chord); *the piano comps round the pulses, not a pad* (at most five strikes a bar, seven in ten on a pulse or the eighth before one, a push in a fifth of the working bars). **Against the fit** (the two charts in the library, no data): the three added failed (the bass quiet one root a bar; the ride without the twelve's triplets (the fit rides each pulse with the skip on the backbeat pulses only, `x..... X...x.`, in six as in twelve); the piano pushed in 0 of 879 working bars); the restated passed.

**The tests**: jazz's fourteen standards now count with its own charts in `few` (`test/takes.ts`), so its charts in a meter other than four are played in every kind on the first seed on a push, as every other style's are; the full run plays all six. Jazz with six charts of its own was counted "few" like the blues and played the waltz and both new charts on two seeds.

**Nothing that exists changed.** Every golden take byte for byte. The eight scorecards, rewritten to a scratch file on the charts their baselines were made on (jazz on its eighteen named), after the last engine and data change: all byte for byte. Jazz's is accepted with the new charts (`score --style jazz --update`).

**The new charts alone, as jazz** (200 takes each). *Ballad in Twelve*: the ride on every beat 97%, the changes stated 100%, the bass's register 37.9, half steps into changes 56%, repeated notes 0%, the piano's lowest note 59.4 (83% inside), colour 90%, no voicing faults; the soloist's changes on chord tones 100%, thirds and sevenths 81%, beats 97%, empty bars 0%, 1.36 and 1.78 notes a second in its first and last chorus. Outside their bands: `solo.cost` 2.83 against 2.32 (no take inside: the slow-twelve reading the rock, blues and funk charts showed), `solo.builds` 1.34 (29% inside), `drums.phrase-end-shot` 59% (60% inside), `bass.notes-per-beat-walking` 1.40 (it counts the pickups as notes a beat; a measure for four). *Jazz in Six*: the ride 100%, the same bass and piano measures inside, `chords.voicing-faults` 1% (78% of takes inside 2%), `chords.top-small-leaps` 75% inside; the soloist 1.86 and 2.52 notes a second, empty bars 13%, cost 1.85. Outside: `solo.builds` 1.36 (20% inside), `bass.notes-per-beat-walking` 1.70.

**Every style on every chart.** The push suite passes. On six seeds every style passes, but the nine processes together cannot all be held on this machine while another builder runs (6 GB; funk's or disco's process is lost with no output, and the start commit's run loses disco the same way at the same time); funk and disco pass their six-seed runs together on their own. Each of the eight styles played each new chart as its own (a copy of the repo with only that chart in its library, under each style's name, its tempo held inside the style's range; every kind on six seeds and the soloist's kinds). Beyond the floors a one-chart library cannot meet:

- *Acoustic on the chart in six*, held quiet with no bassist (bar 21, F#m7b5): a finger under the thumb. Fixed (the engine change above).
- *Funk on the chart in six*: "the bass plays the section's riff, not the kick's roots", a jam of two passes, seed 1, bar 56 (a fill bar at 0.77): roots alone. The fault found on reggae's chart in six at the same bar; funk's, not changed by this branch.
- *Every style's soloist on a one-chart library of either chart*: "the soloist is not sparse" (each style's floor over its own charts: on the ballad at 66 jazz plays 1.71 notes a second, rock 1.27, funk 1.48) and "the player's setting moves the soloist all the way along" (reggae, neo-soul and acoustic dip from 0 to a quarter on the ballad; jazz's share sounding dips at three quarters on the six, 0.633 to 0.598). Both are measured over a style's own charts; on its whole library each passes, jazz's at every quarter on the six-seed run.

**Found, not fixed:**

- *The six-seed run is at the machine's memory*: with nine style processes at once a process is lost, at the start commit too, while another builder is rendering. Not this branch's (the start loses one the same way); watch it on main.
- *The soloist's slow-bar reading*: jazz's soloist plays the same level held quiet and hot in twelve (1.45 notes a second at each level, measured at 60), since a bar of twelve at a ballad's pulse is a slow bar at every level.
- *The scorecard's walking and comping measures read four*: `bass.notes-per-beat-walking` counts the ballad's pickups as notes a beat, and nothing measures the piano's pushes in compound time; the properties above say what those would.
- *Not checked*: whether the ballad's soloist's `solo.cost` and `solo.builds` are the meter or the slow bar (as for rock's, the blues' and funk's twelve).

**Timings** (the machine shared with another builder whose load ran from 2 to 44; each run alone and beside the commit this branch started from, `253db7c`, in a worktree of it). ## acoustic in twelve (stage 6, during gate 6)

An Opus build from `prompts/meter-compound-style.md` in its own clone on the branch `acoustic128`. Acoustic's four seats carry `meters["12/8"]` beside the 6/8 the owner passed; `WRITTEN` in `test/takes.ts` has `acoustic: ["6/8", "12/8"]`; one chart in twelve. Nobody has heard it: the page for his ear is `out/meter-study-5-acoustic/` (not committed; `src/` has the script and the grid printer).

**Which it is: the slow folk ballad in twelve-eight.** The singer-songwriter's band has a tradition in twelve: the slow folk and country ballad counted in four slow pulses, the guitar picking the ballad's arpeggio over a bass on 1 and 3 or strumming down, down, up through each pulse, brushes or a cross-stick on 2 and 4, the upright on root and fifth. A bar of twelve is a bar of four pulses, so what is written is acoustic's own 4/4 band said in pulses (each beat of four a pulse, "the and" a pulse's last eighth, AU-97, ST-acoustic-04), with six's touch where a pulse of three eighths has something four does not (the eighths on the shaker and the hat all three, the strum's pendulum through a pulse, the middle eighth lighter). Nothing swings and nothing is struck between the eighths.

**What each seat plays** (a bar is twenty-four sixteenths in four pulses of three eighths; only the eighths are struck):

- *Drums.* Four's drummer said in pulses. Quiet, one groove a section: half-time (a soft kick on 1, the cross-stick on 3, the shaker's eighths) (2), the cross-stick on 2 and 4 (2), the shaker alone (1); never a snare; answer bars a soft kick on the last eighth of 3 or of 4. Working: the kick on 1 with a soft one on 3, a soft pickup into 3 or into the next One one section in five each; brushes in 35% of sections (the shaker's eighths with a soft snare tap and the hat foot on 2 and 4), else the cross-stick on 2 and 4 over all twelve eighths on the hat or the shaker; answers on the foot or the hat breathing open on the bar's last eighth. Hot: the same kicks, the snare on 2 and 4, the hat on every eighth, opening softly on the bar's last eighth in 40% of sections; answers as working, or a ghost snare on the last eighth. Derived from six (in the file, `twice`): the shaker's and hat's pulse (`o.g.g.`, `x.o.o.`), the backbeat lines (six's backbeat on its second pulse, twice), and the fills (six's pickups, an eighth or two before the barline, `acousticDrumsInSix.fills`). Written for twelve: the kicks, the quiet half-time, the answers. Measured over 10 seeds of the chart held at a level: 9.5 drum strokes a bar quiet, 17.5 working, 16.8 hot; the kick and the snare on 3.0, 4.8 and 4.8 of the twelve eighths, six at most outside a fill bar.
- *Upright.* Four's lines said in pulses, written (half of this bar is not six's bar: six is in two, a root a bar when quiet): quiet root on 1 and fifth on 3 (2) or the root held (1); working root and fifth (2) or with the root again on the last eighth of 2 (1); hot a note on every pulse, root, root, fifth, fifth (2), or the pickup and two fifths (1). Never a pulse's middle eighth. A lead-in is the last pulse, as four's beat 4. 1.6, 2.5 and 4.0 notes a bar.
- *Guitar.* Picked: the ballad's arpeggio, the thumb on 1 and 3 as on 1 and 3 in four and the fingers up the shape and back over two pulses (thumb, index, middle, ring, middle, index), written; six's "up and over" twice (the thumb on every pulse), derived; and "rocking" (thumb, middle, ring, middle, index, middle, from 1 and from 3), written. With no bassist each plays the Travis thumb on every pulse (the bass, the upper string, the alternate bass, the upper string) with the fingers between. Strummed, the pendulum through each pulse, down, down, up, with six's touch (86, the middle eighth 76, the upstroke 14 lighter): working "ring and roll" (1 and 3 left to ring, 2 with its upstroke, 4 rolled into the next One; written for the bar of four pulses) 39, "rolling" (six's, twice) 26, "in two and two" (six's "in two", twice, which is also four's down on every beat and up on the "and"s of 2 and 4) 13, beside the picked patterns' 42 of 120 as in four and six; hot six's two figures twice, 2 and 4 leaned on with the snare. Quiet always picked; working 30% of bars picked; hot none. 8.1 strokes in a strummed bar working, 10.6 hot.
- *Soloist.* ST-acoustic-26's sung cells said in pulses (a quarter of four a pulse held, its eighths the long-short, where the busy line has a sixteenth the pulse's three eighths); four's levels by name and notes a bar (1.8, 3.4, and 5.2 for the busy level, whose cells carry the triplet), four's shapes; endings land on a long note and leave the last pulse unstruck; pickups four's in pulses (the last eighth 2, the long-short in the last pulse 1, the last two pulses at medium and busy 1); the top note held a pulse and most of the bar.

**Properties.** Restated so they mean something in twelve, held in six and twelve: *in six and twelve the eighths are the band's smallest note* (was six); *the upright* (in six in two as it was; in twelve four's root and fifth: every note on a pulse or a pulse's last eighth, never the middle eighth, at most five a bar, the bass note on the One, quiet nothing else but arrivals, the third pulse's fifth or bass note and the step down into a root a seventh below; from the working level the second half's first pulse always played); *a guitar picks a bar of six or twelve as compound time is picked* (the thumb on the first pulse of each half bar, both pulses in six, 1 and 3 in twelve, no finger under it in that half under its chord); *a guitar strums a bar of six or twelve as a pendulum in eighths* (every pulse struck, the first possibly leant into, an eighth between, every stroke between lighter than its pulse); *with no bassist the picking thumb is the bass* (now in bars of twelve too); *a picked note is a string of the shape* (a floor for twelve). Added: *acoustic: in six and twelve the kit is restraint said in pulses* (the kick on the One and elsewhere only on 3 in twelve or a pulse's last eighth; the snare and cross-stick never on the One or a middle eighth, no snare quiet; where the hat or shaker keeps time, every eighth). **Against the fit** (the chart in the library, no data): all seven failed: the eighths (a 4/4 fill's snare on a sixteenth), the upright (nothing on the third pulse at the working level), the kit (the hat not on the second eighth), the picked shape and the thumb (none picked: the fit never picks in twelve), the picking ("quiet, and strummed") and the strumming ("only the pulses strummed").

**Two properties restated for what they met on the six-seed run, not for twelve's sake:**

- *A plucked string rings never past its chord*: the slow blues in twelve has G7 then G7/B a pulse each, and acoustic now picks it. The guitar stands on its root (`rootOnBottom`), so G7/B is the same grip and its G2 rings on; it does the same in four (probed: a G7 G7/B bar in 4/4 rings G2 through), and no other chart in the library has a slash over the same chord. The property now reads the same chord over another bass note as the same chord. That a guitar with no bassist does not take the B under G7/B is ST-acoustic-16's "with no bassist the grip stands on the chord's bass", which the engine does not do in any meter: left.
- *No finger under the thumb* is asked under the thumb's own chord: in twelve a half bar is two pulses, and a chord arriving on 2 (rock's slow dance turns a chord a pulse) is stated by its own bass.

**Decided here, because the brief and the notes did not settle it:**

- *Tempo in twelve: the default, two thirds of four's, 48 to 80, usually 63; nothing written in `tempo.meters`.* As in six: it keeps the picking hand's eighths at four's speed, and a twelve-eight folk ballad lives at 50 to 70. The chart is at 60.
- *The kick on 3 in twelve*, where six has none: a bar of four pulses has a second strong pulse, which a bar of two does not (SU-D-14), so four's soft 3 comes back.
- *Quiet half-time puts the cross-stick on 3*, as four's does, so the kit property allows the backbeat strokes anywhere off the One and off a middle eighth rather than on 2 and 4 alone.
- *No "old faithful" in twelve*: said in pulses it leaves 3 unstruck, a hole of two-thirds of a second at 60; every working figure strikes every pulse.
- *Fills are six's*: a pickup of an eighth or two before the barline is the same in any bar of dotted pulses (ST-acoustic-10's two and four steps).
- *The chart* (`library/acoustic-ballad-twelve-e.chart`, "Folk Ballad in Twelve"): E, 12/8 at 60, thirty-two bars, verse, chorus, bridge, chorus. Verse `E | A/E | E | B7 | C#m | A | F#m7 B7 | E` (the four over home's bass note, the six minor, the two and five a half bar each); chorus `A | B | G#m | C#m | A | B | E | B7` (from the four, ending on the five to turn back); bridge `C#m | B/D# | E | A | F#m7 | G#m | A | B7` (a bass climbing from the six minor). Acoustic's library was in D, E minor, G, A minor, C and A. No named tune.

**A consolidation, so the push holds its time**: the guitar's bars of six held at a level are rendered on a push for acoustic's own chart in six only (every chart in six on the full run), as `heldTakes` holds the other seats; the upright's held takes are `heldTakes` (they were rendered again by the property, on two seeds of every chart in six). The floors were measured again (a push still sees 62 bars of six picked alone, 379 chords picked, 273 bars picked, 124 quiet, 433 strummed; the upright's quiet bars in six are 31 on a push, so that floor is 20).

**No engine change.**

**`COMPOUND_STRUMS`** counts every strum in a bar of dotted pulses by a guitar that strums eighths in a meter it has written: with twelve it reads 6,003 on a push (floor 1,200) and 31,986 on the six-seed run (floor 3,600).

**Nothing that exists changed.** The goldens are byte for byte. The eight scorecards, rewritten to a scratch file on their own charts with every part in (acoustic on its seven earlier charts named one by one), are byte for byte their baselines. Acoustic's is accepted with the new chart (`score --style acoustic --update`).

**The new chart alone, as acoustic** (200 takes): the kick on the One 100%, the changes stated 100%, the bass's register 33.9 (30 to 40), passing tones 4%; the guitar's lowest note 49.1, no colour, no voicing faults, neck movement 0.57; the soloist's changes on chord tones 100%, beats 88%, phrase ends 91% (86% of takes over 85%), a range of 16 semitones, 0.99 notes a second in its first chorus and 1.15 in its last, no bends. Outside their bands: `solo.builds` 1.18 (21% of takes inside 1.30 to 4.00; the soloist's largest known fault, as on rock's chart in six); `bass.note-length-quiet` 14.6 (73% of takes inside 7 to 16: a bar of twelve at 60 is four seconds, and a quiet note is held a half bar); `solo.entered-early` 2% (a phrase led in seldom: the soloist's pickups live in a breath, and at 60 a phrase's last note leaves little).

**Every style on the chart as its own** (a copy of the repo with only this chart in its library, as `<style>-ballad-twelve-e.chart`, its tempo held inside each style's range in twelve, every kind of take on six seeds and the soloist's kinds): no rule about what is played failed in any style. What failed is what a one-chart library cannot meet: the floors on how many cases a property saw; the rules that name `blues-12-bar-a`; "the biggest fill is kept for where the band steps up" (a one-section-form rule: no case); and the soloist's means over a style's charts ("not sparse", "leads phrases in", "the setting moves the soloist all the way along"). Seen, not a failure of a rule: funk's, disco's and reggae's guitars roll no upstroke on the chart (their floors read 0 upstrokes).

**Every style on every chart.** `pnpm test:full` (and its material one style to a process): every style passes but reggae, whose "the top of a chorus, and of the solo, is led into some of the time" fails: "reggae: none of 12 solos led into". It passes at the starting commit (`15a3f2c`, reggae alone at six seeds: 469 pass). The new chart moves which charts each style is handed, and reggae's first solos counted now reach the twelve at which the property asks for one led in; reggae leads into a phrase almost never (0.2% of phrases, "the soloist's pickups: checked and merged"). A floor met by chance, not a rule broken in what is played; changing reggae's data would move its takes. Not fixed: the merge's call.

**Timings** (the machine shared; the starting commit timed beside the branch each time). Acoustic's own tests alone (`ENSEMBLE_STYLES=acoustic node --test --test-isolation=none test/*.test.ts`): 10.4 and 10.3 s at the start, 10.6 and 10.9 s on the branch (load 1 to 2): 0.2 to 0.6 s more, under the 1.5 allowed (before the consolidation above it was 1.3 to 1.5 more). `pnpm test`: 23.8 and 23.9 s at the start, 23.9 and 24.0 s on the branch, of 24 (load 5 to 11; the second branch run read 24.0 s and was called over the budget): the suite is at its budget at the start commit too. `pnpm test:full`: 63.9 s at the start, 65.8 s on the branch (fails on reggae, above), of 72. `pnpm score` 64.0 s of 120, no movement in any of the eight. `pnpm smoke` 12.3 s of 60.

## what the alto does with a note's pitch (stage 6, during gate 6)

An Opus build from `prompts/horn-moves.md`. Brandon, 2026-11-18, of `out/horn-study/` (`scripts/horn-study.ts`): "i'm leaning toward option 9, the player's choice. sounds most varied and natural of the bunch, in my opinion." Clip 9: the last note of each phrase shaken 30 cents either side, 5 a second, from 0.4 s, growing over 0.6 s; one quick scoop (0.06 s) into a landing; one fall of 6 semitones over a beat after the note's full length. Asked whether a fall should die away as it drops: "i trust your judgment on the fade, especially if you think it'll sound more realistic without being a bunch more work". The engineer's decision: a player's fall is a drop in pitch and in breath together, so the alto's fall dies away over the fall's own length.

**What the engine gained.**

- *A note may go on past its end and die away* (`WrittenNote.fade`, `NoteEvent.fade`: ticks past `duration`). Beside `PitchMove`, not in it: it is the note's loudness, not its pitch, and its length in the rhythm stays `duration`, which is what the band hears of it (the comp that listens reads durations, so nothing it hears moves). Feel passes it on untouched. `timedNotes` lengthens the note by it and gives `fade` in seconds (the last stretch of `length`); the MIDI file holds the note through it and says it as channel expression, controller 11, straight down in sixteen steps over the fade and back to 127 when the note is let go. In audio, `Sound.fade` (seconds) makes the sampler ramp the gain straight down to nothing over the note's last stretch in place of its release, stopping the source 20 ms after; `silence` and a choke on a note part-way down take it out from the level it has reached (computed, since a cancelled ramp would leave it at its starting level).
- *The instrument's way* (`SoloInstrument`): `shake.on` (`held`: every note held as long as the style asks, the guitars, as before; `last`: the last note before a breath), `shake.shortest` (seconds), `scoop` (`below`, `takes`), `fall` (`semitones`, `takes`). Only the alto has a scoop and a fall; the guitars' and the vibraphone's entries gain `on: "held"` (or nothing) and nulls, and play what they played.
- *The style's chances* (`SoloStyle.habits.scoop`, `.fall`), validated as chances. Every style writes both, with the line it was taken from.

**Decided here.**

- **A phrase's last note is the last note before a breath, as heard**: the note whose gap to the next note, as heard after swing, is the breath, or the last of the phrase, or the last strike of the tune. That is what the suite's properties call a phrase, and in a shape like `L E L E` it shakes both ends: each is followed by a breath.
- **The least length shaken is 0.7 s as heard**, not after + grows (1.0 s): the shortest note shaken in clip 9 was the first phrase's dotted quarter at 120, 0.71 s; from 0.4 s that is a turn and a half of the shake, at half its depth. The style's `habits.shake` (beats) is the guitar's and does not apply: clip 9 shook a dotted quarter over a jazz band whose guitar threshold is a half note.
- **Every style's alto shakes.** Every style's notes give a vibrato length (SO-52), so none says no; there is no style switch.
- **A scoop goes into a note struck on a strong beat (the bar's first, or the pulse halfway through it) where the chord changes or where the phrase ends; a quarter note or longer in the rhythm; come to in the line (the note before ends well inside a breath of it, as heard) from below or by a leap of a minor third or more down.** A step down into it is not scooped (it is the usual approach, and clip 9's step down was the one the brief named unusual). A note at a phrase's end may be scooped, and then stays plain (SO-52: no shake; SO-54: no fall); without them, scoops had nearly nowhere to go (blues 0.1 a chorus). Never a pickup, never in the bar the band ends on. A half step under, in 0.06 s, from 0 at the note's start: clip 9.
- **A scoop is drawn once a phrase, at the style's chance, onto one of its candidates** (one at most a phrase, so never the note after a scoop). The notes' numbers are per long note; taken here per phrase, they give 0.02 to 0.6 scoops a chorus.
- **A fall goes off the last note before a breath when it is a half note or longer in the rhythm and a dotted quarter or longer as heard, above the line's home (instrument home plus the style's lift), with the band at its working level or above (the seat's energy from 0.42), not scooped, and only where the drop and a breath after it are over before anything else can be struck: the next note of the phrase, or, after its last, the middle of the phrase's last bar, the earliest a pickup into the next phrase can start (SO-37: half its bar at most).** Never in or into a bar the band ends on, never past a take's end. That last rule makes the fall's room the phrase's own: nothing it decides waits on the phrase after, so a jam made longer or told to finish plays every earlier fall as it would have.
- **SO-54's weights are kept**: the chance half as much again in a section's last two bars, seven tenths elsewhere, a third straight after a line that fell. Engine constants (SO-54 gives them for every style); the base chance is the style's.
- **A fall is timed in seconds: 0.5 s**, the beat of clip 9 at 120. A breath and a lip let go at their own speed: at 240 a beat is 0.25 s, a drop of six semitones in it is a smear; at 66 a beat is 0.9 s. The room it needs is in seconds too.
- **The fade is straight in gain, from the note's level to silence at the bottom of the fall**, over the same 0.5 s as the drop; the pitch eases (smoothstep) as every glide does. The shake, on a note that has one, goes on through the drop, as clip 9 drew it.
- **The moves are drawn after the critic has chosen the phrase**, keyed by place (the phrase's first bar for the scoop, the note's place for the fall), so the choice of phrase, every note, and every other draw are what they were.
- **A phrase said again is not said with the same scoops.** The guitar's bends are remembered with the idea; a scoop here is drawn by place. Left for an ear to ask for.
- **`soloDensity` does not move the chances.** It moves which phrases are played, and so which notes there are to move.

**Chances written** (`habits.scoop` a phrase, `habits.fall` a phrase ending that can take one):

- jazz 0.15 (ST-jazz-37) and 0.3 (the notes give jazz none; he chose the fall over the jazz band; SO-54's horn styles' 0.3);
- blues 0.15 (ST-blues-41) and 0.3 (ST-blues-43, SO-54);
- funk 0.2 and 0.35 (ST-funk-32, SO-54);
- disco 0.3 and 0.3 (ST-disco-25);
- neo-soul 0.35 (ST-neosoul-33, SO-53) and none (the notes give none);
- reggae 0.25 (ST-reggae-27, SO-53) and none;
- acoustic none and none (ST-acoustic-28: no scoops; a sung line);
- rock none (ST-rock-24: no scoops) and none.

**Measured** (`out/horn-moves/src/count.ts`: each style's own charts, about 200 takes each, a jam of four passes with the alto, so a three-chorus solo; per chorus 1, 2, 3; a phrase ending is a note with half a second of air after it, or the last):

| style | ending notes | shaken | scoops | falls |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| jazz (27.8 bars) | 13.9, 11.1, 11.0 | 4.6, 3.7, 3.9 | 0.45, 0.33, 0.32 | 0.06, 0.15, 0.07 |
| blues (12) | 7.4, 5.5, 5.2 | 6.1, 4.3, 4.5 | 0.15, 0.20, 0.27 | 0.10, 0.06, 0.07 |
| funk (19) | 14.3, 9.5, 9.0 | 6.3, 5.8, 5.3 | 0.02, 0.07, 0.14 | 0.17, 0.29, 0.47 |
| disco (22.7) | 10.0, 8.1, 7.8 | 7.5, 6.0, 5.7 | 0.44, 0.45, 0.59 | 0.15, 0.21, 0.34 |
| neo-soul (20.6) | 13.5, 11.8, 11.1 | 10.7, 8.4, 8.2 | 0.29, 0.37, 0.52 | none |
| reggae (24) | 15.7, 15.8, 15.3 | 12.7, 11.6, 10.3 | 0.13, 0.20, 0.07 | none |
| rock (22) | 10.1, 9.3, 9.1 | 7.6, 6.8, 7.1 | none | none |
| acoustic (20) | 10.4, 8.8, 8.8 | 8.9, 7.6, 8.1 | none | none |

- **Falls are rarer than SO-54's chance reads**: of the endings, 1 to 5 in a hundred fall. Few endings can take one: most are short, low, or have no half second plus a breath before the next phrase can start. A three-chorus jazz solo has a fall one time in four; funk's, about one. Clip 9 had one in eight bars. At double the chance (for his ear, below): jazz 0.2 to 0.4 a chorus, the blues 0.1 to 0.4.
- **Scoops are fewer than the guitar's bends in the styles that bend** (on the driven guitar, same charts: the blues 1.9, 0.8, 2.3 a chorus; disco 0.6, 0.6, 1.1), except neo-soul's second chorus (0.37 scoops against 0.05 bends; over the solo 1.2 against 1.4). Jazz, funk and reggae do not bend.
- **A ballad shakes more than it scoops** everywhere: shakes are on most endings long enough, scoops one a phrase at most.

**Properties.** One added (`test/soloist.test.ts`, "a horn moves a note's pitch as a player does, and seldom"), reading the takes the soloist's properties already read, rendering none: the alto shakes only a note followed by a breath, with clip 9's shake, on a note of 0.7 s or more; at most one scoop a heard phrase, a half step in 0.06 s, after no breath, from below or a leap, on a strong beat, never in the ending bar, the note plain; a fall only off a note followed by a breath, a dotted quarter or longer, starting at the note's end, 6 semitones in 0.5 s, dying away over the same ticks, never sounding into the next note as heard, past a closed take's end, or into a bar with the band's ending; no note of any seat dies away but a falling alto note; per style, shaken endings a fifth of all at least, scoops and falls present where the chances are over none and absent where none, scoops under half the chance over the phrases heard, falls under three twentieths of the chance over every ending. On the starting commit it fails in every style ("0 of 1582 phrase endings shaken, 0 scoops ..., 0 falls" in jazz). Broken in the engine one at a time, it failed on: the alto shaking every held note; a fall without its fade; a scoop after a step down; every candidate in a phrase scooped; a fall's room taken away and its drop made 2 s ("a fall into the next note"); the room and the ending checks taken away ("in the bar the band ends on", disco; "too many falls", the blues); falls ten times as likely ("too many falls", funk and disco; jazz's eligible endings are too few to cross its ceiling); scoops five times as likely ("too many scoops", jazz and disco). Taking the room check away with the drop as built did not fail it: half a second of breath after the note already holds a drop of half a second.

The guitar's property ("a guitar bends only into ...") no longer asks the alto to be plain: it skips an instrument that shakes `last`, and its floor on plain notes is the vibraphone's, asked only where a style's own soloist is the vibraphone.

**Unchanged, and how it is known.** `out/horn-moves/src/fingerprint.ts`, run on the starting commit (a worktree of `0c6f249`) and on the branch: every library chart in every style, on each of the seven solo instruments, as a jam of four passes on seeds 1 and 2, the song and an open jam of three on seed 1 (13,632 takes, 2,505,544 solo notes): the drums', bass's and chords' events the same in every take; the guitars' and the vibraphone's solo events and MIDI bytes the same in every take; the alto's notes (all but `move` and `fade`) and their count the same in every take.

**Goldens.** Rewritten, the four whose soloist is the alto: `disco-four-chord-c-solo`, `funk-two-chord-dm-solo`, `jazz-rhythm-changes-bb-solo`, `reggae-three-chord-d-solo`: the solo track gains pitch bend and, where a note falls, expression; its notes are the same 232, 210, 401 and 79. The drums, bass and chords tracks are byte for byte; the other thirteen goldens (four with a guitar or the vibraphone soloing, nine with no soloist) are byte for byte.

**Scorecards**: written before and after to scratch files for all eight styles: identical.
